Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-10-24 (record the difference, do not mirror the SDK's presence check: a stray empty variable must not break a working credential setup).

Call-site markers: `ai/providers/bedrock/bedrock.go` (`loadBedrockConfig`, the bearer token selection).

Evidence: `TestBedrockBearerTokenDivergencesFromPi` (`ai/providers/bedrock/bedrock_bearer_divergence_test.go`) runs Pi's `bedrock-converse-stream` through the Node oracle and PiG's provider against a loopback server with the variable empty, under skip-auth and under static keys: Pi sends nothing and ends with the signing error, PiG sends a SigV4 request; its controls (variable unset, with keys) agree. The test fails when either side changes.

Parity allowance: none needed beyond that test. The paired Bedrock oracles (`TestBedrockRequestBodyMatchesPi` and the others) scrub every `AWS_*` variable before they run Pi, so they do not see the difference.

Remove when: Pi or its AWS SDK treats an empty `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK` as unset (Pi passes `authSchemePreference` itself, or the SDK tests for a non-empty value), or an owner decision reverses this one; then the marker, the D195 cases of the test and this record go.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D196 Under skip-auth a bearer token from the process environment is not sent over an http:// endpoint, where Pi's AWS SDK sends it

What: with `AWS_BEDROCK_SKIP_AUTH=1` and a non-empty `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK` in the process environment, and a model `baseUrl` that is `http://` (a local proxy or a gateway that "needs no authentication"), PiG's request fails before it is sent with "bearer token with HTTP request requires HTTPS". Pi 1.4.0 sends it with `Authorization: Bearer <token>` over plain HTTP. In both products the AWS SDK selects bearer auth from the variable although `AWS_BEDROCK_SKIP_AUTH=1` asked for dummy keys (Pi: `packages/ai/src/api/bedrock-converse-stream.ts:185-191` sets no `config.token` under skip-auth, `@aws-sdk/core` `NODE_AUTH_SCHEME_PREFERENCE_OPTIONS.environmentVariableSelector` picks `httpBearerAuth` and `@aws-sdk/token-providers` `fromEnvSigningName` reads the variable; PiG: `resolveEnvBearerToken`, `third_party/awssdk/service/bedrockruntime/api_client.go:980-984`). The difference is the signer: PiG's own bearer path installs `bedrockBearerSigner`, which sends the header over HTTP as Pi's SDK does, but only when `loadBedrockConfig` set a token, which it never does under skip-auth, so the SDK's default signer applies and refuses a request that is not HTTPS (`smithy-go` `auth/bearer/middleware.go:97`, `SignHTTPSMessage.SignWithBearerToken`). Without skip-auth, or over `https://`, PiG and Pi send the same header.

Why: the refusal keeps a bearer token off an unencrypted connection. Pi's behavior sends it in clear text to a host the user chose; PiG does not override the Go SDK's guard to repeat that.

Pi bug: Pi sends a credential over plain HTTP because the JavaScript SDK has no HTTPS check for bearer tokens, and its skip-auth option does not stop the SDK from picking up the token.

Scope: `AWS_BEDROCK_SKIP_AUTH=1` with `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK` non-empty in the process environment and an `http://` `baseUrl`, on the `bedrock-converse-stream` API. A token passed in the provider environment, as `bearerToken` or as the API key, is not read under skip-auth by either product's own code, and takes PiG's HTTP-tolerant path when skip-auth is off.

Workaround: none needed in PiG. Use an `https://` endpoint, or unset the variable. In Pi, the same two steps avoid sending the token in clear text.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-10-24 (record the difference, do not mirror: PiG keeps the Go SDK's HTTPS-only bearer guard where skip-auth leaves the token to the SDK).

Call-site markers: `ai/providers/bedrock/bedrock.go` (`loadBedrockConfig`, the bearer token selection; `bedrockClientOptions`, the bearer signer).

Evidence: `TestBedrockBearerTokenDivergencesFromPi` (`ai/providers/bedrock/bedrock_bearer_divergence_test.go`) runs Pi through the Node oracle and PiG's provider against an `http://` loopback server with skip-auth and a token: Pi's request arrives with `Authorization: Bearer tok`, PiG sends nothing and ends with the HTTPS error; its control (a token without skip-auth over `http://`) sends `Bearer tok` in both. The test fails when either side changes.

Parity allowance: none needed beyond that test. The paired Bedrock oracles scrub every `AWS_*` variable before they run Pi.

Remove when: Pi refuses to send a bearer token over HTTP (its SDK adds an HTTPS check, or Pi installs a signer that does), or PiG installs `bedrockBearerSigner` under skip-auth and the owner approves sending the token over HTTP there; then the markers, the D196 case of the test and this record go.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D197 The env daemon's limit of open files is the hard limit, where Pi's daemon keeps the soft limit it inherited

What: PiG's env daemon (`env/daemon`) runs under the Go runtime, which raises the soft `RLIMIT_NOFILE` of the process to the hard limit (on Linux to one below it, `syscall/rlimit.go`) before `main` starts. Pi's daemon (`packages/env/daemon`, Rust) never touches the limit, so it runs under the soft limit the client's shell passed on. On a default shell (soft limit 1024) Pi's daemon answers an `open`, `write` or `opendir` with `EMFILE` once about 1020 handles are open (each handle holds one descriptor), where PiG's daemon goes on until the hard limit or its own handle table (4096 handles, the same `EMFILE` Pi's daemon reports at that count, `packages/env/daemon/src/main.rs:42-43` and `:116-120`) stops it. A client that opens fewer handles than the soft limit sees no difference. The commands the daemon starts are not affected: Go restores the inherited limit for a child process.

Why: nothing in PiG reaches the limit on purpose. The Go runtime's raise is the toolchain's own behavior (every Go program on Unix has it), and undoing it to give a program Pi's smaller limit would make PiG fail on workloads that succeed, in a daemon whose handle table already caps the count.

Pi bug: none. Pi's daemon leaves the limit alone (no `setrlimit` call in any file of `packages/env/daemon/src`) and maps the system's `EMFILE` as it should (`src/sys/unix.rs:35`). The two counts differ because the two runtimes differ.

Scope: Unix hosts only (Windows has no such limit), and only a daemon started with a soft limit below both its hard limit and 4096. On macOS the Go runtime raises the soft limit to the hard limit or `kern.maxfilesperproc`, whichever is lower. Everything else, including the handle table limit and its message, is equal to Pi's (`TestDaemonHandlesAreLimitedLikePisDaemon`).

Workaround: none needed in PiG. Pi's count cannot be reproduced in PiG: the runtime raises the limit again whatever the daemon inherits.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-10-24 (record the difference, do not lower the limit to Pi's).

Call-site markers: `env/daemon/server.go` (`Serve`, where the daemon's connection starts).

Evidence: `TestDaemonDescriptorLimitIsTheHardLimitUnlikePisDaemon` (`env/daemon_descriptor_limit_divergence_test.go`) starts Pi's daemon (built from the pin) and PiG's with a soft limit of 64 and the host's hard limit, and opens directories until one fails: Pi's refuses with `EMFILE` before 64, PiG opens all 300. `TestDescriptorLimitIsTheHardLimit` (`env/daemon/descriptor_limit_linux_test.go`) pins that the runtime leaves PiG's soft limit at the hard limit or one below it. The first fails when either daemon changes its behavior, the second when the runtime stops raising the limit.

Parity allowance: the wire tests that open thousands of handles (`TestDaemonHandlesAreLimitedLikePisDaemon`) start both daemons with the soft limit raised to the hard limit (`startWireDaemon` in `env/daemon_wire_oracle_test.go`), so the count under test is the handle table's, equal on both sides.

Remove when: the Go runtime lets a program keep its inherited soft limit, or Pi's daemon raises its own soft limit to the hard limit at start, or an owner decision reverses this one; then the marker, the two tests and this record go.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D198 The Go, Rust, Python and Lisp `Type` has no Codec family (`Codec`, `Decode`, `Encode`, `DecodeBuilder`, `EncodeBuilder`, `IsCodec`)

What: pi-ai's `Type` is TypeBox 1.3.27's builder namespace. The Go (`sdk.Type`), Rust (`pig_sdk::Type`), Python (`pig_sdk.Type`) and Lisp ports serve its schema constructors and guards, but not the six members that attach a JavaScript decode/encode callback pair to a schema (`Codec(type).Decode(f).Encode(g)`, `Decode(type, f)`, `Encode(type, g)`, the `DecodeBuilder` and `EncodeBuilder` classes, and the guard `IsCodec`). They are absent: a call fails to compile in Go and Rust and raises `AttributeError` in Python, and the Lisp SDK defines no such function. The Node runtime serves TypeBox's own and behaves as in Pi.

Why: a Codec's callbacks have no consumer outside `Value.Decode` and `Value.Encode` (`typebox/build/value/codec/callback.mjs`, the only reader of `~codec`), which pi-ai does not export (`ai/src/index.ts` exports `Static`, `TSchema` and `Type` from typebox) and no SDK serves. Pi's own tool-argument validation does not call them (at Pi b556cfe5, `ai/src/utils/validation.ts:15,59` compiles the schema with pi-schema and calls `parse`). A port could store the callbacks, but nothing in any SDK or in the host could ever call them, which is an inert member that accepts the call and does nothing.

Pi bug: none.

Scope: the six members above only, in `extensions/sdk/type_schema.go`, `extensions/sdk-py/pig_sdk/type_schema.py` and `extensions/sdk-rs/src/type_schema.rs`. Every other member of `Type` is ported and tested against Pi's own answers (`test/parity/sdk-vectors/pi-ai/Type.json`); the schemas `Type` builds are plain JSON on the wire, so a schema that a Node extension builds with a Codec registers the same JSON it would without one (`~codec` is a hidden member that JSON drops).

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-10-24 (Codec family of `Type`: numbered divergence; `Refine` and `IsRefine` are not part of it: the SDKs run an extension tool's refinements themselves).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Amendment 2026-11-02 (owner decision of the same day): the approval above (2026-10-24) was given when the SDKs ran an extension tool's refinements themselves. Pi 1.5.0 no longer runs them (`ai/CHANGELOG.md:25`, `coding-agent/docs/extensions.md:176`: "`Type.Refine()` refinements are not run"), and this branch removed the SDK refinement round trip to match. `Refine` and `IsRefine` therefore stay schema constructors whose refinements no tool-argument validation runs, as in Pi. They are not part of D198: the owner approved a separate record for them, D226 (Go, Rust, Python and Lisp `Type.Refine` store a callback that no API runs).

Remove when: an SDK serves `Value.Decode`/`Value.Encode` (or a host call runs a codec) so a stored callback pair has a consumer.

Call-site markers: `extensions/sdk/type_schema.go` (`Type`), `extensions/sdk-py/pig_sdk/type_schema.py` (module doc), `extensions/sdk-rs/src/type_schema.rs` (module doc), `extensions/sdk-lisp/src/type-schema.lisp` (file header).

Evidence: `TestTypeOmitsTheCodecFamily` (`extensions/sdk/type_vectors_test.go`), `test_type_omits_the_codec_family` (`extensions/sdk-py/tests/test_pi_ai_type_vectors.py`) and `type-omits-the-codec-family` (`extensions/sdk-lisp/tests/type-tests.lisp`) require that the six members do not exist; the Rust members are absent by construction (`type_schema.rs` doctest `compile_fail`). The Node row of `TestConformance_TypeSchemaToolParametersAgreeAcrossSDKs` (`test/extension-conformance/type_schema_registration_test.go`) runs TypeBox's own `Type`.

## D199 PiG lists OpenRouter's :nitro and :floor routing variants beside every OpenRouter model, where Pi lists only what OpenRouter's /models returns

What: for every `openrouter` chat model of Pi's catalog that does not already end in a routing variant, PiG also lists `<id>:nitro` and `<id>:floor` (named `<name> (nitro)` and `<name> (floor)`), right after the model, and resolves a lookup of either. The variants are derived from the base entry when listed or looked up, from the current OpenRouter list: the bundled catalog, or the list `pig update --models` saved, which replaces it (the saved list never holds a derived variant); the bundled catalog (`ai.GeneratedModels`) stays exactly Pi's. A variant is a copy of its base entry in everything but its id, name and JSON record: API, base URL, prices, limits, input types, reasoning and thinking levels are the base model's. `--list-models`, the model selector, `/scoped-models`, RPC `get_available_models` and the SDKs list them like any other built-in model.

Why: OpenRouter accepts `:nitro` (providers sorted by throughput, priority service tier endpoints admitted) and `:floor` (providers sorted by price, flex service tier endpoints admitted) on every model ID but never lists them in `GET /api/v1/models`, which is where Pi's catalog comes from, so a user cannot pick them in Pi's selector (issue #315). OpenRouter's variants page (https://openrouter.ai/docs/guides/routing/model-variants/overview, "Offering routing variants in a model picker") tells clients to derive them from the base entries and to read the metadata from the base entry.

Scope: one rule, `ai/openrouter_variants.go` (`OpenRouterRoutingVariants`, `OpenRouterVariant`, `WithOpenRouterRoutingVariants`, and `WithOpenRouterRoutingVariantModels` for a saved list), data-driven from OpenRouter's documented suffix list; no model is named. Used by `ai.ListModels` (OpenRouter and the whole catalog), `ai.LookupModel` and `ai.LookupModelExact` (a variant the catalog does not list is derived from its base and cached), `builtinChatCatalogListed` (the registry's selector, `--list-models`, RPC and SDK lists and `RuntimeModels`; built on first use, so a caller that only resolves a model, or reads the available models of a registry without OpenRouter auth, builds none), `GetProviderModelDataByID` and the model reference resolver (`withNamedRoutingVariants`: a reference that names `:nitro` or `:floor` finds the variant of the model it means). A partial model text (`--model 4o`, scoped-model patterns, the resolver's fuzzy match) is not matched against a variant unless the text itself names `:nitro` or `:floor`, so every text Pi resolves resolves to the same model here (Pi's reverse-collation tie break would otherwise prefer `:nitro`). The listed price is the base model's rate; OpenRouter bills a request at the rate of the endpoint that served it (a priority endpoint for `:nitro`, a flex endpoint for `:floor`). Image and classifier models, other providers, the remote catalog overlay and the other routing variants (`:exacto`, `:online`) are unchanged.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-10-24 (issue #315, an additive extension that overlaps Pi's catalog).

Call-site markers: `ai/openrouter_variants.go` (`OpenRouterVariant`), `ai/registry.go` (`ListModels`, `LookupModel`, `LookupModelExact`), `internal/codingagent/builtin_chat_catalog.go` (`builtinChatCatalogListed`), `internal/codingagent/model_catalog_data.go` (`GetProviderModelData`, `GetProviderModelDataByID`), `internal/codingagent/remote_catalog_provider.go` (`withPersonalizedCatalogOverlay`), `internal/codingagent/model_pattern.go` (`withNamedRoutingVariants`, `fuzzyMatches`).

Evidence: `TestRefreshedOpenRouterProviderListsRoutingVariantsOfTheRefreshedList`, `TestSavedOpenRouterListShowsRoutingVariantsOnEveryRegistryListing` (`internal/codingagent/remote_catalog_openrouter_variants_test.go`) and `TestRegistryPersonalizesTheOpenRouterCatalogThroughTheConfiguredBaseURL` pin the refreshed list; `TestOpenRouterRoutingVariantsMirrorTheirBaseEntries`, `TestOpenRouterRoutingVariantsAreLookedUpFromTheirBase` and `TestRewriteRecordIdentity` (`ai/openrouter_variants_test.go`) pin the rule from OpenRouter's documentation: both variants after each base entry, equal to the base but for id, name and record, found by lookup, none on another provider, none stored in `GeneratedModels`; `TestAvailableModelDataDerivesRoutingVariantsOnlyForAConfiguredOpenRouter` and `TestSelectorModelLookupBuildsOnlyTheModelRead` (`internal/codingagent`) pin that a variant is derived only where it is listed or named, and `TestPartialModelTextIgnoresOpenRouterRoutingVariants` (`internal/codingagent/model_pattern_openrouter_variants_test.go`) pins the resolver rule against the failure the paired `TestResolveCliModelAndScopeMatchPi` showed when a variant was a candidate (`--model 4o` resolved to `:nitro`, Pi to `:batch`).

Parity allowance: the bundled catalog and every comparison of it with Pi (`TestCatalogModelJSONMatchesPiForEveryBuiltinModel`, `TestGeneratedCatalogsMatchPublishedPackage`, `TestModelObjectsMatchPisModelObjects`) are unchanged. The tests that compare a provider list with Pi's (`TestBuiltinCatalogsMatchPinnedProviderDataModules`, `TestProviderCatalogExportsMatchPinnedData`, the Anthropic catalog denominators, `TestModelRegistryMatchesPi`) leave the derived variants out of the comparison. `TestResolveCliModelAndScopeMatchPi` is unchanged and still equal to Pi.

Remove when: Pi lists the routing variants (or OpenRouter lists them in `/models`), or the owner reverses the decision; then the derivation in `ai/openrouter_variants.go`, its markers, the allowance in the oracle test and this record go.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D200 With no home directory PiG still runs the commands that load no resources, where Pi crashes at import, and fails with Pi's error where Pi's home lookup would run

What: on a host where Node's `os.homedir()` throws (no `HOME`, and no entry for the process's user in the system's user database: a container run as a uid its passwd file does not list), the pinned Pi 1.4.0 fails to load in every mode, `--version` and `list` included. It exits 1 with Node's uncaught `SystemError [ERR_SYSTEM_ERROR]: A system error occurred: uv_os_homedir returned ENOENT (no such file or directory)`: `tools-manager.ts:11` evaluates `getBinDir()` at import, `getAgentDir()` expands even an explicit `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` through `normalizePath`, and `normalizePath` calls `homedir()` before it looks at its input (`utils/paths.ts:89`). PiG runs `--version`, `list` and every other command that loads no resources, and fails with the same message (exit 1, nothing on stdout) only where Pi's `resolve()` would look the home directory up, `getHomeDir()` at `package-manager.ts:2464`: startup in print, RPC and interactive mode, a reload and `pig config`.

Why: PiG's config root (`PIG_HOME`, else `XDG_CONFIG_HOME/pig`, else `~/.pig`, internal/configroot) is meant to run without a home directory, as containers and CI jobs do; refusing `--version` and `list` there would remove the one thing `PIG_HOME` is for, and nothing in those commands needs a home directory.

Pi bug: none claimed; Pi's behaviour is a crash at load rather than a reported error, and is kept for the paths that need a home directory.

Scope: `PackageManagerHomeDir` (`internal/codingagent/resource_paths.go`) is Pi's `getHomeDir` and returns Node's error where Node's call throws (`internal/nodehome`); `ValidateAutoDiscoveredOverrides` returns it first, at the position of `package-manager.ts:2464`, so startup, a reload and the config command fail there with exit status 1 and nothing on stdout. The collectors (`AgentsSkillScan`, `AmbientSkillResources`) list no user `~/.agents/skills` when asked without a home directory. PiG's other lenient home lookups (`resolvepath`'s `~` expansion, `configroot.ExpandTilde`) are not part of this record and unchanged. The record's condition is the host's, not an option: with a home directory PiG and Pi behave alike.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-10-24 (keep `--version`, `list` and resource-free modes running where Pi crashes at import; fail with Pi's error where Pi's home lookup runs).

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/resource_paths.go` (`PackageManagerHomeDir`, `ValidateAutoDiscoveredOverrides`).

Evidence: `TestNoHomeDirectoryPiCrashesAtImportWherePiGRunsResourceFreeCommands` (`coding/cli/no_home_directory_test.go`) runs the pinned Pi (`pi-coding-agent/dist/cli.js` with `os.homedir()` made to throw Node's error, so it runs on any host) and requires exit status 1, Node's message on stderr and an empty stdout for `--version` and `list`, then runs PiG's `--version` and `list` with no home directory and requires them to succeed. `TestStartupFailsWhereNoHomeDirectoryExists` requires print and RPC startup to exit 1 with that message and an empty stdout; `TestStartupFollowsNodeWhenThereIsNoHomeDirectory` runs the binary against `node -p "require('os').homedir()"` with `HOME` removed on the host itself, and on a host with no passwd entry for the uid (checked on xd670: Pi `--version`, `--mode rpc` and `-p` all exit 1 with the SystemError, PiG `--version` and `list` exit 0 with `PIG_HOME` set).

Parity allowance: `TestNoHomeDirectoryPiCrashesAtImportWherePiGRunsResourceFreeCommands` is the allowance: the Pi half pins the crash, the PiG half pins the run. No paired corpus runs without a home directory, so none changes.

Remove when: Pi stops resolving the home directory at import (so `--version` and `list` run there without one), or an owner decision reverses this one; then PiG's startup fails at the same point as Pi's, the markers, the allowance test and this record go.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D203 registerProvider with an empty `apiKey` leaves the provider unconfigured where Pi treats the empty string as a configured key

What: Pi's `registerProvider(id, { apiKey: "" })` (the extension registration of `ModelRuntime` and `ModelRegistry`) keeps the empty string as the provider's configured raw key: `configuredApiKey` is `extension?.apiKey ?? config?.apiKey` (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/provider-composer.ts:397`), and `""` is not `undefined`. `configuredRequestAuthStatus` then reports `{ configured: true, source: "fallback" }` (`provider-composer.ts:723-736`, the `"fallback"` source is the extension's literal key), and the composed API-key `check` returns a configured check (`provider-composer.ts:450-457`: an empty key has no environment names to miss). After the availability refresh settles, `hasConfiguredAuth(provider)` is true, `getProviderAuthStatus` reads configured, and the provider's models are available. PiG reads an empty `apiKey` as no key (the registration's `APIKey` is a Go string, and `""` means unset on every path that reads it): the provider stays unconfigured and its models unavailable until a stored credential, an environment variable or a non-empty key supplies one.

Why: Pi's behaviour is an accident of `??` on a string. The empty string is not a form the option documents (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/extensions/types.ts:1936`: "API key literal, env interpolation ($ENV_VAR or ${ENV_VAR}), or leading !command"), and Pi treats `""` as missing where it validates the same option (`provider-composer.ts:318`, `!config.apiKey`). Its effect is a provider that reads configured and available whose every request fails with `No API key for provider: <id>` (for example `packages/ai/src/api/openai-completions.ts:95`), so an extension that passes `process.env.KEY ?? ""` gets a selectable provider that cannot answer. Distinguishing `""` from an absent key needs presence tracking on the public `ProviderConfigInput.APIKey` string and on the models.json path, for a quirk no caller can use.

Pi bug: the inconsistency above: `""` is a configured key to `configuredApiKey`, `configuredRequestAuthStatus` and the composed `check`, and a missing one to `provider-composer.ts:318`.

Scope: the extension registration (`ModelRuntime.RegisterProvider`, `ModelRegistry.registerProvider` over every SDK, `ProviderConfigInput.APIKey`) and the configured-auth reads that follow it: `ConfiguredRequestAuthStatus`, the registration's request auth, and `mergeProviderConfigInput`, whose merge keeps the earlier key when a re-registration passes `""` (Pi's merge keeps the earlier key only for `undefined`, `model-runtime.ts:921-933`). A non-empty key, `$VAR` and `!command` keys behave as in Pi.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-10-25 (record, no port).

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/model_configured_auth_status.go` (`ConfiguredRequestAuthStatus`), `internal/codingagent/extension_model_registry.go` (the registration's request auth and its configured status), `internal/codingagent/native_model_runtime.go` (`mergeProviderConfigInput`).

Evidence: `TestRegisterProviderWithEmptyAPIKeyIsNotConfigured` (`coding/register_provider_empty_apikey_test.go`) registers one provider with `APIKey: ""` and one with a literal key: after the refresh settles the first is unconfigured and its status unconfigured, the second configured. Pi's side was read through the session-graph `ModelRegistry` over Pi's own `ModelRuntime` (the Node row of `TestSessionGraphModelRegistryAcrossSDKs`): `{ configured: true, source: "fallback" }`, available `true`, for `apiKey: ""`.

Parity allowance: no parity scenario registers a provider with an empty `apiKey`.

Remove when: Pi treats `""` as missing in `configuredApiKey`, or the SDK API gains explicit presence for `apiKey` (a pointer or an option) and an owner decision asks for Pi's behaviour; then the three markers, the test and this record go.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D205 Fullscreen frames scroll the rows that only moved

What: when the transcript grows by a line or is scrolled, every row of the fullscreen viewport changes, though all but a few rows only moved. Pi 1.4.0, whose default `tuiMode` is fullscreen, clears and writes every row that differs from the previous frame (`packages/tui/src/tui-alt-screen.ts` `doRender`). PiG finds the distance at which the most rows of the changed part of the screen are rows of the previous frame. It scrolls that part inside a scroll region (`CSI top;bottom r`, then line feeds at its bottom or reverse index at its top, then `CSI r`) and draws only the rows that differ after the move. A frame scrolls only when this saves at least three row writes and no row of the moved part holds an image, because images are placed by their own sequences. The screen after every frame is the one Pi's frame shows; the bytes differ. A 20,000-character answer streamed into a 160x48 tmux pane made PiG write 3,578 KB to the terminal; with rows scrolled it writes 544 KB, and the tmux server's CPU for that answer falls from 0.97 s to 0.23 s (medians of four runs). `PIG_TUI_SCROLL_ROWS=0` draws every changed row, as Pi does.

Why: the bytes are work for the terminal, and traffic for a multiplexer or an ssh connection between PiG and the screen. The technique comes from Pi-Bolt (MIT; see THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md).

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-10-27, contingent on terminal-compatibility proof, and confirmed the same day with that proof. The proof: a terminal PiG does not trust with scroll regions (`TERM` unset, `dumb`, `unknown` or a terminal with no scroll-region capability such as `vt52`, or Zellij) and `PIG_TUI_SCROLL_ROWS=0` get Pi's bytes (`scrollRegionsUsable` in `tui/tui_alt_screen_scroll.go`); the tmux parity families that touch the alternate screen (`interactive-rendering`, `fullscreen`, `tui-components`) pass with their comparators unchanged; `make test-term-engines` shows the same screens and scroll margins with and without scroll regions in xterm.js, alacritty_terminal, wezterm-term, libvterm and pyte; the owner compared both by hand in Ghostty, Terminal.app and Tern.app; and Windows conhost gets Pi's bytes. The owner accepted landing without a Windows Terminal run: native Windows consoles leave `TERM` unset and so get Pi's bytes, and `PIG_TUI_SCROLL_ROWS=0` restores them everywhere else.

Call-site markers: `tui/tui_alt_screen.go` (`doRender`), `tui/tui_alt_screen_scroll.go` (`minRowsSavedByScrolling`).

Evidence: `TestAltScreenScrolledFramesShowPisScreens` (`tui/alt_screen_oracle_test.go`) replays Pi's bytes and PiG's for every step of the 500 scenarios of the pinned Pi's alternate-screen fixture on a terminal model with scroll regions, and requires equal rows after every step; 55 steps scroll. `TestAltScreenFramesMatchPiOracle` still compares the bytes, with scrolling off. `TestAltScreenScrolledFramesShowEveryRow` (`tui/tui_alt_screen_scroll_test.go`) draws random frames (lines added, extended, deleted, inserted and changed, the viewport scrolled, the dock changed) and requires the terminal model to show each frame's rows. `TestAltScreenScrollsTheRowsThatMoved`, `TestAltScreenScrollsRowsDownWhenScrolledBack`, `TestAltScreenDoesNotScrollImageRows`, `TestAltScreenDoesNotScrollAnImageRowThatStays` and `TestAltScreenScrollRowsOff` cover the moves, images and the switch. `BenchmarkAltScreenFrameWhileTheTranscriptGrows` writes 153 bytes per frame against 5,417.

Terminal-engine evidence: `make test-term-engines` (`docs/testing/term-engines.md`) replays a deterministic corpus (streamed transcripts, wheel and PageUp scrolling, resizes, wide and styled text, overlays, random edits and the pinned fixture's scenarios) twice, as the scroll-region frames and as Pi's bytes (`PIG_TUI_SCROLL_ROWS=0`), through xterm.js, `alacritty_terminal`, `wezterm-term`, libvterm and pyte, and requires the same screen (rows, styles, shown cursor) and the whole-screen scroll margins after every frame in every engine. Removing the region reset after a move fails all five.

Known visible difference (accepted by the owner on 2026-10-27): a component that renders a row wider than the screen. Pi clips such a row after it has added the row's closing SGR reset (`applyLineResets`, then `sliceByColumn(line, 0, width, true)` in `packages/tui/src/tui-alt-screen.ts` `doRender`; `applyLineResets` is in `tui.ts`), so the reset is cut off with the clipped text and the row's attributes stay on in the terminal; Pi's own recorded bytes show this (`tui/testdata/alt-screen-oracle.json.gz`, scenario 485 step 3: a component renders a bold row 157 columns wide, and Pi writes its clipped text followed directly by the next row's cursor move, with no reset). The attributes color the rows written after the clipped row, so which rows show them depends on which rows a frame writes. Pi writes every changed row and PiG's scroll frames move the others, so where the leaked attributes appear differs between the two (`known-clipped-sgr-leak` in `tui/alt_screen_term_corpus_test.go`, which the engines must keep showing; fixture scenarios 173 and 485 in `termLeakingOracleScenarios`). Rows that fit the screen never leak, and with the clipped rows closed by a reset the same scenarios are equal in all five engines. The Pi-identical bytes (`PIG_TUI_SCROLL_ROWS=0`) are unchanged.

Fallback evidence: `TestAltScreenScrollFallsBackToPisBytes` (`tui/alt_screen_oracle_test.go`) replays the 500 scenarios with `TERM` unset, `dumb`, `vt52` and `unknown`, and with `PIG_TUI_SCROLL_ROWS=0` in an xterm, and requires each step's bytes to equal Pi's exactly; `TestScrollRegionsUsableIn` lists which terminals are trusted, Zellij included.

Parity allowance: the fullscreen parity scenarios compare the captured screen, which is the same, and the alternate-screen oracle compares Pi's bytes with scrolling off and Pi's screens with it on.

Remove when: Pi scrolls the rows that moved, or an owner decision reverses this one. Then every changed row is drawn again, and the markers, `tui/tui_alt_screen_scroll.go` and this record go.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D206 The Durable core resolves HarnessOptions.settings once before each event, where Pi resolves it at each use

What: Pi calls `HarnessOptions.settings` at each use: the scheduler, a generation's phases, the context retention checks, the queue modes of a submission and `resolveAgent` each resolve it (`packages/durable/src/harness/harness.ts`), so a settings callback sees every resolution (`harness-restart-failure.test.ts` counts 49 reads of `extensions` for a round of one generation with one tool call). The Durable core reads the settings from the snapshot the host sends before each event it delivers (`durable/core/host/cf/src/facade/harness.ts` `syncSettings`; `sqlhost` `Options.Settings` for the Go host), and only `resolveAgent`, which stays with the host, resolves the callback at its use. The callback is therefore called once per event, and a change of the settings takes effect at the next event rather than at the next use within an event. The host reads no `extensions` for that snapshot, so the test above counts PiG's 6 agent resolutions of a round where Pi counts 49.

Why: the core runs in a Wasm module with no import to call the host's callback at each use; a host call at each use costs a crossing at every phase, retention check and queue-mode read, and a settings notice that made the host call the callback as often as the core read it (ABI 12) cost an ABI change and a count per event for a callback no consumer observes by its count.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-10-28.

Call-site markers: `durable/core/host/cf/src/facade/harness.ts` (`syncSettings`), `durable/core/turnhost.go` (`turnSettings`), `durable/core/sched.go` (`contextRetentionMs`).

Evidence: the `U-harness-restart-failure` row of `durable/contract/corpus.toml` states the measured value (`measured_test`, `measured_failure`, `measured_divergence = "D206"`): the candidate fails Pi's own test "resolves the settings no more often than before" with `expected 6 to be 49`, and the row fails if it passes or fails otherwise (`durable/contract/lib/gate.mjs`; `vitest-paired.test.mjs` pins the three cases). `TestCoreTheRestartLimitOnTheNormalPath` (`durable/core/driver/harness_restart_failure_core_test.go`) counts the calls of the settings source over the core for the same round and asserts 9 a round, where Pi's callback is called 49 times; `TestTheRestartLimitOnTheNormalPath` (`durable/harness/harness_restart_failure_test.go`) asserts the reference Harness's 15 + about 34 resolutions, as Pi's 49.

Parity allowance: the measured row above is the allowance; no other row changes.

Remove when: the core can read the settings by a host call at each use without a measurable cost (the count budgets of `make durable-counts`), or Pi resolves the settings once per step. Then the snapshot, `syncSettings` and this record go.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D207 TuiAltScreen.children is a copy in the Go, Rust, Python and Lisp SDKs, where Pi's is a live array

What: Pi's `TuiAltScreen.children` is the container's own array: a caller that holds it sees every later change of the screen's children, and can change them by writing to it. The Go (`TuiAltScreen.Children`), Rust (`TuiAltScreen::children`) Python (`TuiAltScreen.children`) and Lisp (`tui-alt-screen-children`) SDKs return a snapshot of the children as the SDK knows them after its last `addChild`, `removeChild` or `clear`. Writing to the snapshot changes nothing, and the snapshot does not follow later changes: read `children` again after the call that changed it. `addChild`, `removeChild` and `clear` are the only ways to change the children; the SDKs have no `setChildren`.

Why: the renderer, with the screen's child list, runs in the host process and the extension's components in the extension's process, so an array in the extension cannot be the renderer's array. The SDKs keep the children they sent to the host and update that list after each call returns; a list a caller could write to would need a call to the host on every write and a way for the host to change the extension's list, which Pi's array is not.

Pi bug: none claimed.

Scope: `TuiAltScreen.children` of the Go, Rust, Python and Lisp SDKs. Every other member behaves as Pi's, and a read after `addChild`, `removeChild` or `clear` returns what Pi's `children` holds then, in order, with equal-content components kept distinct and a duplicate child kept twice.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-10-28.

Call-site markers: `extensions/sdk/tui_alt_screen.go` (`Children`), `extensions/sdk-rs/src/tui_alt_screen.rs` (`children`), `extensions/sdk-py/pig_sdk/tui_alt_screen.py` (the `children` property), `extensions/sdk-lisp/src/tui-alt-screen.lisp` (`tui-alt-screen-children`).

Evidence: `TestTuiAltScreenChildrenAcrossSDKs` (`test/extension-conformance/tui_alt_screen_test.go`) reads `children` after each `addChild`, `removeChild` (of a child and of a component that is not one), a duplicate `addChild`, `removeChild` of the first of two equal entries and `clear`, with three components of equal content, and requires the order Pi's own class reports in the Node cell, in every isolated and packed SDK cell.

Parity allowance: no paired Pi corpus holds `TuiAltScreen.children` across a change of the children, so none changes.

Remove when: Pi's `children` stops being a public live array, or an owner decision asks for a live view across the process boundary; then the four markers, the evidence note and this record go.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D208 From inside a component's render, a TuiAltScreen of the Go, Rust, Python and Lisp SDKs fails the calls that re-enter its frame

What: Pi runs a component's `render` on the one thread that draws the frame, so a render that calls a member of its screen is a nested call, and Pi lets it recurse or re-enter: `renderNow()` and `render()` render the document again inside the frame, `start()` and `stop()` nest a terminal start or stop, `copyActiveSelectionToClipboard()` and `queryTerminalColors()` start Promise-returning work from inside the frame, and the `onInput` callback that `Terminal.start` received dispatches input from inside it. In the Go, Rust, Python and Lisp SDKs every other call a render makes to its screen runs at once, while the host holds the frame lock for that render, and a later read in the same render sees it, as in Pi. These six calls and the input callback would take the frame lock again, which the frame holds and does not release for the render it waits on: from inside a callback the frame waits for (a component's render, an overlay's `visible` callback, a style callback, an `invalidate` or focus change the frame triggered) they return the host's error `tui.alt: <op> cannot run while a component renders`, and the screen goes on. Called from any other thread they wait for the frame, as before.

Why: the renderer takes one non-reentrant lock for the whole frame, and a re-entered frame would need it while the frame waits for the render that re-enters it, which is a deadlock; supporting Pi's recursion needs a frame that renders again inside itself, which the renderer's single pass and its differential state do not allow.

Pi bug: none claimed.

Scope: the calls `render`, `renderNow`, `start`, `stop`, `copyActiveSelectionToClipboard`, `queryTerminalColors` and the terminal's `onInput` callback, made from a callback the frame waits for, in the Go, Rust, Python and Lisp SDKs (the host serves `tui.alt` calls; the SDKs report the host's error as the call's error). The Node SDK serves Pi's own class and recurses as Pi does.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-10-28, for the six calls and for the terminal `input` callback.

Call-site markers: `coding/extension/host/subprocess/tui_alt_bridge.go` (`run`).

Evidence: `TestTuiAltScreenFrameReentryAcrossSDKs` (`test/extension-conformance/tui_alt_screen_test.go`) calls each of the six from inside a render in every isolated and packed Go, Rust, Python and Lisp cell, requires the error for each, and requires the screen to answer the next call in the same render (`setShowHardwareCursor` then `getShowHardwareCursor`).

Parity allowance: no paired Pi corpus constructs `TuiAltScreen` through an SDK and recurses in a render, so none changes.

Remove when: the host's frame can be entered again from inside a render and these calls run as Pi's do; then the call-site marker, the error and this record go.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D212 `/llama` explains a 404 from llama-swap's missing load and unload endpoints, where Pi shows "llama.cpp returned HTTP 404"

What: Pi's `/llama` loads and unloads a model with `POST /models/load` and `POST /models/unload` (`extensions/llama/client.ts`, `load` and `unload`) and shows an HTTP error as the server's `error.message`, else `llama.cpp returned HTTP <status>`. llama-swap lists its models at `/models` with a `status.value`, so the `/llama` list renders, but it has no load or unload endpoint of that shape: it loads a model when a request names it in `model`, and Pi's `/llama` ends in the bare `llama.cpp returned HTTP 404` (reported as #380). When `/models/load` or `/models/unload` answers 404, PiG asks whether the server is llama-swap, by the `"src": "llama-swap"` of the 404's error envelope or a `GET /running` that answers `{"running": [...]}`, and if it is, shows: `/llama cannot load or unload models on llama-swap: it loads a model when a request names it. Add llama-swap to models.json as an openai-completions provider with baseUrl <server>/v1, then pick models with /model. See <docs link>.` Any other 404, and every other status, keeps Pi's text. `GET /running` is the one request PiG sends that Pi does not, and only after such a 404.

Why: Pi's text names neither the server nor the way that works, and the reporter took it for a PiG bug. The way that works is the models.json provider, where PiG and Pi behave the same (journey `llama-swap-models-json`); `/llama` stays a llama.cpp router manager.

Pi bug: none claimed.

Scope: the `Load` and `Unload` calls of the llama.cpp client, so the `/llama` load, unload, restore-after-failed-load and stop-loading paths. `Download` (`POST /models`) and every other request keep Pi's text.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-10-29.

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/llama/client.go` (`explainLlamaSwap`).

Evidence: `TestLlamaClientAndCatalogAgainstLlamaSwapAndLlamaServerMatchPi` (`internal/codingagent/llama/llamaswap_test.go`) runs Pi's client and PiG's against a fake llama-swap and a plain llama-server, requires Pi's `llama.cpp returned HTTP 404` from Pi for load, unload and unload-and-wait on both, the llama-swap message from PiG on llama-swap, Pi's text from PiG on the plain server, and every other outcome and request equal. `TestLlamaCommandAgainstLlamaSwapExplainsTheMissingEndpoints` runs the `/llama` command, `TestLoadAndUnloadRecognizeLlamaSwapByItsErrorEnvelope` the envelope, and `TestLoadAndUnloadKeepPisTextForA404ThatIsNotLlamaSwap` and `TestLlamaCommandKeepsPisTextForARouter404` the 404s that keep Pi's text.

Parity allowance: `TestLlamaClientRequestsMatchPi` compares load and unload against Pi over random statuses and bodies, 404 included; its server answers `GET /running` with 404, which is not llama-swap's, so every outcome there stays Pi's.

Remove when: Pi's `/llama` names llama-swap itself, or can manage llama-swap models; or the owner withdraws the decision. Then the call-site marker, the `GET /running` probe, the docs section and this record go.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D213 A crashed or exited extension runtime releases the read the host holds for an autocomplete provider chain

What: Pi's editor awaits an autocomplete request in the Promise jobs that follow the terminal read that typed the text (`editor.ts` `requestAutocomplete`, `startAutocompleteRequest`), and its extensions run in Pi's own process. A provider chain that needs no I/O therefore opens its menu before the next read, and a chain that awaits real I/O (a timer, a socket, a process) has not settled when the next read arrives. PiG's extension chain crosses a process boundary, so after each read PiG starts the request of the extension chain and holds the next read until the chain settles and its menu is published, or until the chain announces that it awaits real I/O (`ui.autocomplete.pending`), which is when Pi's next read would have come first. The Node runtime announces at the end of its macrotask (`setImmediate`), Pi's rule exactly; the base provider's awaited work (an `@` file search, a command's Promise-returning argument completer) is announced by the host, also through a chain another packed member calls directly. The Go, Rust, Python and Lisp SDKs have only synchronous providers, which settle when they return, as a synchronous Pi provider holds the loop until it returns, so they announce nothing. No timer releases the read. The one difference a user can see: when the extension runtime exits or its connection ends while the chain is unsettled, the request fails and the host releases the read; in Pi a crashing extension takes the whole process with it.

Why: the hold is the mechanism that carries Pi's event-loop order across the process boundary. A runtime that dies cannot answer, and a frozen editor would be worse than Pi, where the process would have ended.

Pi bug: none claimed.

Scope: requests of the extension provider chain that start at the end of a read: natural slash, argument and mention requests of text typed or pasted. Debounced symbol requests (a later task in Pi too), forced Tab requests and a request made behind an unfinished one never hold the read. The native provider chain without an extension provider is unchanged.

Owner decision: owner Michael Kinsy ordered Pi's exact rule for 0.6.0 on 2026-10-29 ("don't care about delay, most correct") and a numbered record for what remains.

Call-site markers: `coding/extension/host/subprocess/autocomplete_bridge.go` (`GetSuggestions` of the remote provider).

Evidence: journey `extension-completion-fast-enter` (j18, packed Node runtime) runs Pi and PiG through `/model provider/id` and `/thinking level` typed and entered at once. `TestAutocompleteSettlementAcrossSDKs` runs a fast chain, a chain that sleeps and a chain that waits on a slow host answer in the Go, Node (isolated and packed), Rust, Python and Lisp realizations, fused and packed Go included, and requires the sleeping chain to be announced in Node only. `TestNodeAutocompleteChainTellsTheHostWhenItAwaitsIO` pins the Node rule in isolated and packed mode and `TestPackedNodeMembersShareTheBaseProvidersIOAnnouncement` the packed case. `TestEnterInTheReadAfterTheTextAcceptsTheMenuAnExtensionProviderOpened`, `TestEnterWaitsForAnExtensionProviderThatBlocksUntilItReturns`, `TestEnterInTheReadAfterTheTextSubmitsWhileAnExtensionProviderAwaitsIO` and `TestBaseProviderAnnouncesItsAwaitedWork` cover the host, and `tui/editor_autocomplete_hold_test.go` the editor.

Parity allowance: none needed for the journey: a provider that needs no I/O matches Pi, and no journey can observe a runtime exit.

Remove when: Pi's extensions run outside the editor's process, or the owner withdraws the decision. Then the release on runtime exit, the call-site marker and this record go.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D215 A local Package typed by absolute path is stored as typed

What: Pi's `pi install <path>` records every local Package in `packages[]` relative to the settings directory as it was spelled at install (`package-manager.ts` `normalizePackageSourceForSettings`). An install made through one spelling of the agent directory (a symlinked `PIG_HOME`, such as an XDG link) writes, for example, `../../../../../../pkgsrc/my-pkg`; a start through another spelling at another depth resolves it to a path that does not exist, and the Package's tools are gone. PiG stores a path typed absolute (`/abs/pkg`, `~/pkg`, a `file://` URL) as typed, trimmed; a path typed relative (`./pkg`, `../pkg`) is still stored relative to the settings directory, as Pi stores it.

Why: 20 of 21 Packages on one host disappeared after a re-registration run through a symlinked `PIG_HOME`, with only a boot line on stderr to show it (#389). An absolute path resolves the same way from every spelling of the agent directory.

Pi bug: Pi writes the same relative record (`package-manager.ts` `normalizePackageSourceForSettings` at the pin, b3a29f11); not reported upstream.

Scope: `PackageManager.NormalizeSourceForSettings` (`pig install`, `pig remove` matching is unchanged: both forms resolve to the same path), through `resolvepath.IsAbsolutePackagePath`. Entries already in `settings.json` are read as before; running `pig install <absolute path>` again rewrites an entry that still resolves to the absolute form. The SDK resource loader is unchanged.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-10-30 (store an absolute path as typed rather than always absolute or symlink-resolved relative). The report of a Package that does not resolve is not part of this record: Pi skips it silently, so it is the additive D216.

Call-site marker: `internal/packagemanager/package_manager.go` (`NormalizeSourceForSettings`).

Evidence: `TestInstalledAbsoluteLocalPackageResolvesThroughAnotherAgentDirSpelling` (`coding/cli/package_local_path_test.go`, red with the call site reverted), `TestNormalizePackageSourceForSettings_UsesScopeBase` (`coding/cli/package_commands_test.go`), `TestPackageManagerAddAndRemoveSourceInTheScopeSettings` (`internal/packagemanager/package_manager_ops_gaps_test.go`: an absolute spelling of a relative entry replaces it with the absolute path); `TestSessionGraphDefaultPackageManagerAcrossSDKs` and `TestSessionGraphLispPackedAcrossSDKs` (`test/extension-conformance/session_graph_test.go`: the script installs an absolute path, so every PiG row reports `<root>/pkgs/localpkg` where the Node row, Pi itself, reports the settings-relative spelling).

Parity allowance: the ported case `package-command-paths.test.ts:431` ("allows local package install to initialize fresh project settings", `TestPortWave10PackageCommandPaths`) installs an absolute path and expects the entry relative to `.pi`; PiG's port asserts the path as typed. Scenario `cli-utils/04-package-install-local` installs a relative path, which is stored as Pi stores it.

Remove when: Pi stores an absolute local path as typed, or the owner reverses the decision. Then the branch, the call-site marker and the tests' D215 cases go.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D218 Chord's HTTP listener runs on Go's net/http, which answers some malformed requests before the component does

What: Pi's Chord HTTP listener is Node's `http` module; PiG's is a `net/http.Server` (`chord/nextnode/http`). Pi's parser hands a request with two `Host` headers, or a `Host` with userinfo, to the component, which refuses it 403 with the default security headers. Go's server refuses both itself, before any handler, with a canned `400 Bad Request` and none of the default headers (`net/http/request.go` "too many Host headers", `server.go` "malformed Host header"). Also: an HTTP/1.0 request's answer has an `HTTP/1.0` status line (Node writes `HTTP/1.1`); the status line's reason phrase is Go's (the answers' own text is Node's table); header names are written in canonical case; and net/http watches the connection for a disconnect only once the request body is read, so a client that goes away with an unread body is seen when the route reads or writes, where Node sees the socket close at any time.

Why: no `http.Server` option, hook or `ErrorLog` reaches these checks, and a pre-scan or rewrite of the request bytes would be an HTTP framing parser. The listener keeps Node's limits (`headersTimeout`, `requestTimeout`, 16 KiB of headers, keep-alive 5 s) in Go's settings.

Scope: the listener of `chord/nextnode/http` (`node.go`). `Fetch` and `NodeRequestOf`, which take a request from any server, still refuse these requests with Pi's 403. Every affected request is refused either way and no route runs, so the difference is visible only to whoever owns the listener.

Owner decision: 2026-11-01, owner approved recording the net/http differences as one numbered divergence (D218).

Call-site marker: `chord/nextnode/http/node.go` (the "Forced Go differences" block).

Evidence: `TestTargetsRefuseAnAbsoluteFormTargetTwoHostHeadersAndNone` and the other tests of `chord/nextnode/http/node_test.go` state both answers; the review tests in `review_test.go` pin the cancellation reason `http: the client disconnected`.

Parity allowance: none needed; the Pi side has no oracle that sends these requests to a Go listener.

Remove when: Pi's listener stops being Node's `http` module, or Go's net/http lets a handler see a request it now refuses.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D219 pi-schema regular expressions differ from V8 in four contrived cases

What: PiG's `schema` package compiles `pattern` and `patternProperties` keys and the `regex` format with `schema/internal/ecmaregex`, which translates an ECMAScript pattern to `dlclark/regexp2`; Pi runs V8 (`packages/schema/src/shared.ts`, `formats/typebox/regex.ts`). A schema whose pattern uses a case-insensitive modifier group `(?i:...)` (ES2025; Node 22 rejects it, so Pi on Node 22 throws at compile) with a backreference, or one of three Annex B patterns, or a subject V8 stores two bytes per unit although it holds only Latin-1 units, can accept or reject a string that Pi rejects or accepts. No pattern outside modifier groups and no u-mode pattern without `(?i:)` differs; every other row of the 3,821-pattern golden agrees with V8.

Why: V8 compares a backreference inside `(?i:...)` by ECMAScript Canonicalize; regexp2 compares with `unicode.ToLower`, and a translation cannot change a comparison that runs inside regexp2. The three Annex B results have no deterministic rule in V8's regexp source. V8 decides some modifier-group results by whether the subject string is stored one byte per unit, which is not a function of the string value, so PiG uses "every unit is <= U+00FF".

Scope: `schema/internal/ecmaregex` (the case-insensitive backreference translation in `emit.go` and `casefold.go`), reached through `schema.Compile` and `formats.IsRegex`. Oracle: Node 24.22.1.

Owner decision: 2026-11-01, owner approved recording the four differences as one numbered divergence (D219).

Call-site markers: `schema/internal/ecmaregex/emit.go`, `schema/internal/ecmaregex/casefold.go`.

Evidence: `TestKnownGaps` (`schema/internal/ecmaregex/golden_test.go`) pins each row on both sides, so a change of either side fails it; the golden replay (`TestGoldenRegex`) covers every other row.

The differences, in modifier groups with a backreference:

| mode | pattern | input | V8 | PiG |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| u | `^(?i:(\w)\1)$` | `"s\u017f"` | true | false |
| u | `^(?i:(s)\1)$` | `"s\u017f"` | true | false |
| u | `^(?i:(\u017f)\1)$` | `"s\u017f"` | true | false |
| u, annexB | `^(?i:(\u03c3)\1)$` | `"\u03c3\u03c2"` | true | false |
| u, annexB | `^(?i:(\u03c2)\1)$` | `"\u03c2\u03c3"` | true | false |
| u, annexB | `^(?i:(.)\1)$` | `"\u03c3\u03c2"`, `"\u03c2\u03c3"` (and `"s\u017f"` in u) | true | false |
| u, annexB | `^(?i:(\w)\1)$` | `"i\u0130"` | false | true |
| u, annexB | `^(?i:(i)\1)$` | `"i\u0130"` | false | true |
| u, annexB | `^(?i:(\u0130)\1)$` | `"\u0130i"` | false | true |
| u, annexB | `^(?i:(.)\1)$` | `"i\u0130"`, `"\u0130i"` | false | true |
| annexB | `^(?i:(\w)\1)$` | `"k\u212a"` | false | true |
| annexB | `^(?i:(k)\1)$` | `"k\u212a"` | false | true |
| annexB | `^(?i:(\u212a)\1)$` | `"\u212ak"` | false | true |
| annexB | `^(?i:(.)\1)$` | `"k\u212a"`, `"\u212ak"` | false | true |

Annex B patterns with no deterministic V8 rule (they need `(?i:...)` with Annex B syntax, a lone `{`, `\a`, `\c`, `\ude00`, and only arise when the u-mode compile of the same source fails):

| mode | pattern | input | V8 | PiG |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| annexB | `(?i:[\n\p{Lu}\x62]\|[^a-c😀x-z])\W\u0061` | `"A\u03c3a\u00b5"` | true | false |
| annexB | `(?im-s:(?<n1>(?:)()\s{\|\u212a[\n\0K]()?)(?:()))` | `"\u212ak"` | false | true |
| annexB | `(?ims:(?m:(?=\aΩ\c){,2}\n)(?<n1>\b(?=xx??)\d)\|\/(\w)[aA]\|[\u0061a-c\cA])+[^\ude00]` | `"AB"` | false | true |

These need `(?i:...)` with Annex B syntax (a lone `{`, `\a`, `\c`, `\ude00`); they only arise when the u-mode compile of the same source fails.

One-byte subject storage: `(?i-:[\-c\D]?\u212a{2})` on `"kK"` gave both results in one V8 run depending on how the subject was built; there is no PiG result to compare.

Parity allowance: `schema/internal/ecmaregex/golden_test.go` (`TestKnownGaps`) states both sides of every row.

Remove when: regexp2 gains Canonicalize-based case-insensitive backreference comparison, or PiG gets its own backtracking engine for the ECMAScript grammar. Then the markers, the rows of `knownGaps` and this record go.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D220 Markdown renders each paragraph with the inline state at its position, not with the lines of an earlier identical block

What: Pi 0f21a331 (`fix(coding-agent,tui): reuse syntax highlighting and rendered Markdown blocks`) renders a top-level Markdown block again with the lines of a block of the same source (`blocks.get(token.raw) ?? previousBlocks?.get(token.raw)`, `packages/tui/src/components/markdown.ts:381`). The earlier block can come from the same render or from the previous render of the component; the block is reused when its width and the type of the next block match too (`markdown.ts:383`). `reuseBlocks` (`markdown.ts:373`) turns the reuse off only for a text that defines reference links. marked's inline lexer carries state from block to block: an open raw `<a>` tag suppresses URL autolinks until its `</a>`. The same source therefore renders differently at different positions, and Pi keeps the lines of the other position. Two cases, observed against Pi b556cfe5:

- Same render: Pi autolinks `http://a.b` in `"http://a.b\n\nx <a href=\"u\">open\n\nhttp://a.b\n\nclose</a>"` inside the open `<a>`, because an earlier identical paragraph was autolinked. With `http://a.c` in that place it is plain.
- Previous render: after rendering `"a\n\nhttp://a.b\n\nz"`, `setText("x <a href=\"u\">a\n\nhttp://a.b\n\nz</a>")` keeps the autolinked `http://a.b` line. A fresh Pi component for the second text renders it plain.

PiG renders each block with the lexer state at its position, as Pi did before 0f21a331 and as Pi does when no block has the same source. A component given new text renders the same rows as a new component.

Why: Replicating the Pi behavior makes the output depend on which identical blocks came earlier and on the history of the component (streaming order, edits before the block). Pi's own reference-link guard (`reuseBlocks`, `markdown.ts:373`) exists to avoid that property; it guards reference definitions and not the `inLink` and raw-block state. PiG keeps reuse of syntax highlighting by language and code (the rest of 0f21a331), which does not depend on inline state.

Pi bug: not reported upstream. The repro is `TestMarkdownBlockReuseDiffersFromPi` (`tui/markdown_render_oracle_test.go`): both cases above, run against the pinned Pi, with the rows Pi and PiG render.

Scope: `tui.Markdown` rendering (`tui/markdown_blocks.go`, `renderBlocks`). Only a block that has a block of the same source and width in the same render or the previous one, and whose inline lexer state differs on entry (an open raw `<a>`, so a bare URL stays plain in PiG and is linked in Pi), renders differently. Every other document renders identically to Pi.

Owner decision: 2026-11-01, owner approved recording this as a numbered divergence (D220).

Call-site markers: `tui/markdown_blocks.go` (`renderBlocks`), `tui/markdown_render_oracle_test.go` (`TestMarkdownBlockReuseDiffersFromPi`).

Parity allowance: `TestMarkdownBlockReuseDiffersFromPi` (`tui/markdown_render_oracle_test.go`) runs the pinned Pi for both cases and a control, and states both sides of each row: Pi autolinks the URL inside the open `<a>` and PiG does not. It fails when either side changes.

Evidence: the same test, which needs the Pi oracle (`make install-oracle`); every other document is compared with Pi by `TestMarkdownRenderMatchesPi`.

Remove when: Pi stops reusing rendered Markdown blocks by token.raw across differing inline state (markdown.ts:381). Then the markers and this record go, and the cases of `TestMarkdownBlockReuseDiffersFromPi` become documents of `markdownRenderDocs`.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D222 A plugin input the build cannot read is reported at the import that names it, not at esbuild's library

What: Pi builds plugins with esbuild's JavaScript API. When a plugin's `onLoad` cannot read an input (`readFile` throws `EACCES`), esbuild's JavaScript library takes the error's location from the first readable frame of the JavaScript stack, which is its own `await callback(...)` in `lib/main.js`, and Pi prints `<path of esbuild's lib/main.js relative to the plugin's directory>:1552:22: EACCES: permission denied, open '<path>'` (esbuild 0.28.2). PiG builds on esbuild's Go API, which has no JavaScript stack: the location of an input that cannot be read is the position of the import that names it, so PiG prints `worker.ts:1:19: EACCES: permission denied, open '<path>'`. The text after the location is Node's, as Pi's.

Why: the line Pi prints is a line of a JavaScript library that exists only where Pi's `node_modules` is, and nothing in PiG reads it; printing it would invent a file and a position. No Pi test or example asserts the location.

Scope: `chord/nextnode/plugins` `BuildFacet`, the failure of an input that cannot be read (`ENOENT` and `EACCES` read errors). The build fails in both with one error whose text after the location is equal. Evidence of Pi's text: reproduced with esbuild 0.28.2 in Node (`parseStackLinesV8` takes the first readable frame, esbuild's own `lib/main.js:1552:22`).

Owner decision: 2026-11-01, owner decided to keep PiG's location and record the difference (D222).

Call-site marker: `chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_build.go` (the `OnLoad` read failure).

Evidence: `TestEsbuildBuildAForbiddenInputFailsTheBuildWithNodesText` (`chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_node_unix_test.go`) states PiG's text with the import's location.

Parity allowance: none needed; Pi's text names a file of Pi's installation, so no parity scenario can compare it byte for byte.

Remove when: Pi reports the importing location.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D224 A watch listener that blocks on a wait the host cannot see holds back the waits and publications that follow its delivery

What: Pi runs a watch's drain as a microtask: a listener runs up to its first `await`, and the code that awaited a Harness wait's promise, and the Session's macrotask hop between two commits of one storage call, resume only after that (`packages/chord/src/watch.ts` `#schedule`/`#drain`, `packages/durable/src/session/session.ts` `onJobEnd`, the hop of `writer.ts`). A listener's `await` ends its synchronous part, so nothing ever waits for a listener's own blocking. A Go listener is a blocking call with no await point: PiG's `chord.DeliveryScheduler.AwaitEntered` waits until each earlier delivery's current call returned, or went on to a wait the host provides (the Session's `Park`/`Await`, the line, gate and writer waits, the host loop's park), or ended. A listener that blocks on something the host cannot see (a channel send or receive, a mutex, network I/O, a child process) before it returns has not entered: every Harness wait that follows its delivery (`WaitForTask`, `WaitForIdle`, `Submission.Wait`, `Conversation.WaitForIdle`) and the Session's hop, and with the hop every later publication of that Session, wait until it returns. A listener that sends on an unbuffered channel the test reads only after `WaitForTask` returns stalls `WaitForTask` until its context ends.

Why: Go cannot observe a goroutine's first suspension. The ordering Pi guarantees (a listener sees one call per published part, and its synchronous part ran before the waiting code resumes) needs the delivery to say it went on to a wait; the earlier mechanism that read every goroutine's state gave the order up above 128 goroutines and cost a stack dump per wait. A hop that did not wait for listeners would let a drain fold the frames of several parts into one call, which Pi never does.

Scope: every watch listener (`chord.Watch.Start`, `chord.NativeWatch.Start`, `durable.WatchHandle.Start`, the Harness's conversation, task graph and registry watches), and the waits that call `Session.AwaitDeliveriesEntered` and the Session's `Hop`. The contract: a listener that blocks on a wait the host cannot see calls `chord.DeliveryEntered()` first, or does the blocking on a goroutine of its own and returns. Forgetting the call does not fail; the waits behind that delivery do not return. A listener that only uses the Session's, the Harness's or the host's own calls needs nothing: each of their blocking points is a wait of the Session (`session.Wait`, `Park`, the line and gate waits) and enters the delivery before it blocks.

Owner decision: 2026-11-02, owner approved keeping the deterministic handshake and recording the contract as one numbered divergence (D224).

Call-site markers: `chord/watch_impl.go` (`DeliveryEntered`, `awaitEntered`), `durable/session/session.go` (`Hop`, `AwaitDeliveriesEntered`), `durable/session/writer.go` (the hop between two parts), `durable/harness/scheduler.go` (`WaitForTask`, `WaitForIdle`), `durable/harness/submissions.go` (`Submissions.Wait`), `chord/next/host/host_host.go` (the wait of `Reload`'s report), `chord/next/host/host_link_deliveries.go` (`awaitDelivered`).

Evidence: `TestAListenerThatCallsTheHarnessAtOneOfTwoEndsOfACallDoesNotStallTheHop` (`durable/harness/harness_delivery_blocking_points_test.go`) and the Close and Release cases of `durable/session/wait_delivery_test.go` state that a listener that calls the Session or the Harness does not need the call: `WaitForTask` of the next part's task and `Close` from the listener of the first part return. `TestAListenerThatBlocksOnAChannelStallsHarnessWaitForTaskUntilItCallsDeliveryEntered` (`durable/harness/harness_delivery_contract_test.go`) states both sides on a settled chat: the unmarked listener holds `WaitForTask` until its context ends, the marked one does not. The ports of three Pi tests carry the call because their listener awaits a promise, which Go blocks on: example 21 (`durable/examples/lifecycle_examples_test.go`, the UI listener that awaits `settled`), `TestTaskDocuments/a watch busy in an earlier call when its task ends fails with the Harness before its retirement call` and the other users of `deferredGate.wait` (`durable/harness/task_support_test.go`).

Parity allowance: the harness tests that port Pi's waits and listeners (`durable/harness/wait_deliveries_test.go`, `durable/harness/harness_group_commit_test.go`) run Pi's cases unchanged except for the marked awaits above; `TestAwaitEnteredSeesEveryFrameOfARunningDrain` and `TestGroupCommitTheHopWaitsForTheSynchronousPartOfAListener` state Pi's one call per part.

Remove when: Go can observe a listener's first suspension (a blocked goroutine reports what it waits for to the host without a call of the listener's), or Pi's drain stops running a listener's synchronous part ahead of the code that awaits the commit.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D225 A thread-based SDK's wait for the run it started needs the SDK's yield helper

What: Pi's `pi.sendMessage(..., { triggerTurn: true })` marks the Session busy before its first `await` (`agent-session.ts:2404`, `_startRun` sets `_isAgentRunActive` first, `1895`), and Pi's one event loop runs nothing else until the code that sent the message awaits or returns, so `ctx.isIdle()` reads busy in every extension until then. A handler that waits with `while (!ctx.isIdle()) await sleep(10)` sees the run end, because its `await` is where the loop turns. A Go, Rust, Python or Lisp handler has no `await`, and the Host runs the started run concurrently with the SDK's thread, so the Host cannot tell a raw sleep that stands for Pi's `await` from one that stands for Pi's synchronous wait. The Host answers `isIdle` busy for every extension from the send until the handler returns, calls a member whose Pi API returns a Promise (`waitForIdle`, `exec`, `setModel`, the dialogs and the rest of `returnsPromiseInPi`), or calls the SDK's yield helper (`ctx.Yield()` and `ctx.Sleep(d)` in Go, `ctx.yield_now()` and `ctx.sleep(...)` in Rust and Python, `pig:ctx-yield` and `pig:ctx-sleep` in Lisp), which stands for the `await`. A handler that polls `isIdle` with a raw sleep (`time.Sleep`, `thread::sleep`, `time.sleep`, `sleep`) and no helper keeps the hold and never sees idle until it returns; a send from a thread a handler started after its request returned holds nothing, because no event loop reports where that callback yields. The Node runtime has an event loop and reports its first microtask checkpoint after the send, so it needs no helper and has no difference.

Why: the alternatives are observably worse. Releasing the hold when the triggered turn starts and the Session's busy flag takes over is no release at all, since the Session is busy from the send; it reinstates the defect that a run which ends while the handler still runs makes the next read idle where Pi reads busy. Releasing on a timer is nondeterministic. The helper is the only point where an SDK author can say what Pi's code says with `await`.

Scope: `ctx.isIdle()` after a triggering `sendMessage` in the Go, Rust, Python and Lisp SDKs, isolated and packed, print, JSON and RPC mode. Not affected: a handler that returns, awaits a Promise-returning member or calls the helper; every Node extension.

Owner decision: 2026-11-02, owner ruled "yield helper + divergence": every thread SDK gets the helper with the same interface and behavior, and the residual difference (raw sleep or poll without it, sends from detached threads) is recorded here.

Call-site marker: `coding/extension/host/subprocess/sync_run_hold.go` (`holdAfterSend`, `endSyncRunHold`).

Evidence: `TestReplicatedStateIsIdleStaysBusyThroughTheSynchronousRunAfterATriggeringSend`: `rw_idle_yield_poll` (a `while !isIdle: sleep` poll with the helper sees idle and returns in every SDK) and `rw_idle_blocking` (a raw sleep keeps the hold while the run ends, against the Session's own answer: `after:true` without the hold). `TestSyncRunHoldEndsAtTheYieldCall`.

Related: D224 (`chord.DeliveryEntered`) uses the same pattern for a watch listener's wait; the two mechanisms never meet, since D224 is a package function of Chord's Go packages and D225 a ctx method of a separate extension process.

Parity allowance: none needed; the scenarios that poll with a helper match Pi, and the raw-sleep poll has no Pi counterpart that a thread SDK can express.

Remove when: the thread-based SDKs can report where a handler's thread yields without an explicit call (for example a runtime-level scheduler of their own), or Pi gives `ctx.isIdle()` a definition that no longer depends on the loop's order.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D226 Go, Rust, Python and Lisp serve TypeBox `Value.Check` and `Value.Errors` only, through the host

What: `Type.Refine(type, check, error?)` is a schema constructor in the Go (`sdk.Type.Refine`), Rust (`Type::refine` and `Type::refine_value`, the form for a value that is not a `TSchema`), Python (`Type.refine`) and Lisp (`type-refine`) SDKs. It returns a copy of the schema with the check appended to a hidden `~refine` list, and the schema's JSON is unchanged. As in Pi 1.5.0, nothing runs that list on a tool's arguments; an extension runs it itself, in `execute()`, with TypeBox's `Value.Check(schema, value)` (a boolean) or `Value.Errors(schema, value)` (the errors, a failing refinement's message among them), which Node extensions import from `typebox/value`. The four SDKs serve the same two members: Go `sdk.Value.Check` and `sdk.Value.Errors`, Rust `pig_sdk::value::Value::check` and `errors`, Python `pig_sdk.Value.check` and `errors`, Lisp `value-check` and `value-errors`. They run the refinements in the extension process, in TypeBox's order: the host walks the schema (`internal/typebox` `RefineCheck` and `RefineErrors`, a port of `build/schema/engine/schema.mjs` of typebox 1.3.27) and asks the extension for each refinement call over the `type.engine` host call (`internal/typebox/engine/value_check.go`), so a refinement runs once per call, with the same value, in the order TypeBox calls it. The host keeps the walk parked on a goroutine between the extension's answers, owned by the extension's connection: it is cancelled when the connection closes (a reload, a crash, the end of the session), and the cost is linear in the number of calls. A walk that cycles with no progress into the value (`{"$ref":"#"}`), or is more than 20,000 frames deep (seven times what V8 allows TypeBox), fails with TypeBox's RangeError "Maximum call stack size exceeded"; a value TypeBox can walk, the host walks. A node's refinements run after its other keywords passed; Check stops at the first failure and Errors does not; the operands of `allOf`, `anyOf` and `oneOf` all run; an error a refinement throws (Go: returned, Rust: `Err`, Python: raised, Lisp: signalled) ends the call and is returned or raised as it is.

Why: Pi 1.5.0 validates tool arguments with `@earendil-works/pi-schema`, which runs no TypeBox refinement (`packages/ai/CHANGELOG.md:25`, `packages/coding-agent/docs/extensions.md:176`: "`Type.Refine()` refinements are not run. Check such constraints in `execute()`"). What Pi offers an author for that is `typebox/value` (`docs/extensions.md:161`, "Schema definitions for tool parameters"), whose `Value.Check` runs the refinements. The four SDKs carry no TypeBox, so the host serves the two members for them.

Pi bug: none.

Scope: what is not served, and so differs from `typebox/value` in these four SDKs (Node has the real one): every other member of `Value` (`Parse`, `Assert`, `Clean`, `Convert`, `Create`, `Default`, `Decode`, `Encode`, `Repair`, `Hash`, `Equal`, `Diff`, `Patch`, ... and `Value.Check(context, schema, value)` with a context of named schemas); `Format.Set` formats (a format outside the standard ones is not checked); a `$ref` that does not name a node of the schema itself (`#`, a JSON pointer, an `$id` or an `$anchor` of the schema resolve; `$recursiveRef`, `$dynamicRef` and other documents do not, and such a `$ref` fails the check as an unresolved reference does in TypeBox); and a value that is not JSON (a bigint, `undefined`). A tool's argument behavior is at Pi parity: no refinement runs on the arguments of a tool in any SDK, Node included.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-05 (this text; replaces the 2026-11-02 approval of the earlier record). The 2026-11-02 approval kept tool-argument behavior at Pi parity and planned `Value.Check` after 0.7.0; this record delivers it as row M13 of the 0.7.1 scope.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: every member of TypeBox's `Value` is served by the four SDKs, or Pi stops offering `typebox/value` to extensions.

Call-site markers: `extensions/sdk/type_schema_refine.go` (`Refine`), `extensions/sdk/value_check.go` (`Value.Check`, `Value.Errors`), `extensions/sdk-py/pig_sdk/type_schema.py` (`refine`), `extensions/sdk-py/pig_sdk/type_engine.py` (`Value`), `extensions/sdk-rs/src/type_schema.rs` (`Type::refine`, `Type::refine_value`), `extensions/sdk-rs/src/value.rs`, `extensions/sdk-lisp/src/type-schema.lisp` (`type-refine`), `extensions/sdk-lisp/src/type-engine.lisp` (`value-check`, `value-errors`), `internal/typebox/refine.go`, `internal/typebox/engine/value_check.go`.

Evidence: `TestConformance_RefinedValueRunsRefinements` (`test/extension-conformance/refine_value_test.go`) runs `Value.Check` and `Value.Errors` of the schema of the tool "refine-probe" in the Go, Rust, Python, Lisp, fused Go and packed Node runtimes and requires the same result, errors and refinement calls, in the same order, as TypeBox's own `Value` in the Node runtime, for values with both outcomes (a constant, a skipped refinement or another order fails). `TestRefineMatchesTypeBox` (`internal/typebox/engine/refine_oracle_test.go`) compares the host's walk with typebox 1.3.27 over 49 cases (properties, arrays, tuples, unions, intersections with `unevaluatedProperties`, `not`, `if`, `contains`, `$ref` and cyclic types). `TestConformance_RefinedValueOfASelfReferringSchemaFailsTheCall` requires the RangeError in every SDK and a working host after it. `TestValueWalkCostIsLinearInTheRefinementCalls`, `TestValueWalkFollowsRecursionAsDeepAsTypeBox`, `TestSessionCloseCancelsItsParkedWalks`, `TestAbandonedValueWalksEndWithTheirConnection` and `TestWalkersStopASelfReferringSchema` bound the cost, the depth and the lifetime of a walk. `TestConformance_RefinedToolArgumentsAreNotRefined` still requires that no refinement runs on a tool's arguments.

## D227 A suspended session resumed in the background stays parked until `fg`

What: after Ctrl+Z, a SIGCONT that reaches PiG while its process group is not the foreground group of the terminal on stdin (`bg`, or `kill -CONT` from another process) does not restart the UI. PiG checks `tcgetpgrp(stdin)` first. When the group is in the background it stops itself again with SIGTSTP to the process group, as Ctrl+Z does, without entering raw mode, drawing a frame or touching the terminal, and keeps waiting for the next SIGCONT. `fg` hands the terminal to the group and sends SIGCONT; the check then passes and the UI starts as in Pi. A hung-up terminal answers the check with EIO, which exits 129 as Pi's dead-terminal handling does. When stdin is a terminal that is not the controlling terminal (ENOTTY), no job control applies and PiG restarts the UI as Pi does.

Why: Pi's `handleCtrlZ` (`packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts`, the `process.once("SIGCONT", ...)` handler) calls `ui.start()` and `ui.requestRender(true)` on any SIGCONT. In a background group the termios ioctl of `setRawMode(true)` stops the process with SIGTTOU, so the shell reports `suspended (tty output)` (observed with Pi 1.4.0+b556cfe5, whose `handleCtrlZ` is identical to v1.5.0, on Linux under tmux and bash; the reporter saw the same with an earlier release under zsh). Pi reaches the stopped state only because the terminal call faults: the reporter also sees a first write succeed on a terminal without `TOSTOP`, with a read stopping the process next. PiG stops itself before it touches the terminal, whatever the terminal modes are. The common end state is the same: the job is stopped and `fg` returns to the session; the shell's stop reason differs (`suspended` instead of `suspended (tty output)`).

Pi bug: reported by the user as #396; the reporter's earlier release and 1.5.0 behave the same way. A Pi issue draft is pending.

Scope: only a SIGCONT that arrives while the process group does not own its controlling terminal. Ctrl+Z then `fg`, and every other resume, run Pi's sequence unchanged.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-02 (fix #396 with a foreground check; keep the process-group SIGTSTP target of Pi).

Remove when: Pi checks the foreground process group before it restarts the UI on SIGCONT, or an equivalent that keeps a background resume from touching the terminal.

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/suspend.go` (`suspendTerminal` continuation), `internal/codingagent/interactive_signals_unix.go` (`foreground` operation and the `onSuspendContinue` waiter).
