## D258 Model cycling selects the only model in scope and cycles into the scope from outside it

What: PiG ports Pi main 5732bac06 (`fix(coding-agent): cycle to the only model in scope when the active model is outside it`, #11832, fixing #11806), which is not in Pi 1.5.0. `Session.CycleModel` (scoped and unscoped, forward and backward) now finds the active model's index in the candidate list without a fallback. When the active model is not in the list, forward selects the first entry and backward the last; the result is "nothing to cycle to" only for an empty list or a single entry that is already the active model.

Why: Pi 1.5.0 returns early for a list of at most one model and counts a missing active model as index 0. Ctrl+P with `--models A` and another model active said "Only one model in scope", and with `--models A,B` it went to B, skipping A. Likewise an active model that is no longer available with one other available model.

Pi bug: Pi 1.5.0 (`agent-session.ts` `_cycleScopedModel`, `_cycleAvailableModel`), fixed by Pi main 5732bac06 ([#11806](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/issues/11806)).

Scope: `Session.CycleModel`, `BeginModelCycle`, `BeginModelCycleOnMain` (Ctrl+P, Shift+Ctrl+P, RPC `cycle_model`). The interactive "Only one model in scope/available" message still shows for a nil result.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy for 0.7.1 (SCOPE-PROPOSAL row S17).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: the pin reaches a Pi release containing 5732bac06.

Call-site markers: `coding/session_model_cycle.go` (`nextCycleIndex`).

Evidence: `TestSessionModelExtensionCyclesUpstream` ("cycles to the only scoped model and to the first scoped model from outside the scope", Pi's test), `TestSessionCycleModelSeesExtensionProviderRegistration` (cycle after the active model's provider is unregistered).

Parity allowance: `TestAgentSessionMatchesPi` replays 600 seeded scenarios against the pinned Pi 1.5.0. A replay stops (an explicit stop flag from `runAgentSessionWithPig`, and only the ops before it are compared) at a `cycleModel` op that starts outside its candidate list going forward, or outside a one-entry list in either direction (`cycleDiffersFromPinnedPi`); a backward cycle from outside a list of two or more entries selects the last entry in both versions and stays compared, and so does a forward cycle whose first two entries are the same model with the same thinking level (1.5.0's second entry is main's first). Measured: 137 of 600 scenarios stop, and 3629 of 4520 ops are still compared with Pi. The test logs those numbers on every run.

## D261 SSE lines end at CR, LF or CRLF

What: PiG's SSE reader (`mcp.ConsumeSSEStream`) ports Pi main b64e6badd (`fix(mcp): recognize CR-only line endings in Streamable HTTP SSE`, #11775, fixing #11764), which is not in Pi 1.5.0. A line ends at CR, LF or CRLF, as the SSE specification says. A CR that ends the buffered bytes ends its line at once (the event is delivered while the stream is open), and the LF that may start the next read is skipped, so a CRLF split across reads is one line ending. Pi 1.5.0 splits on LF alone and strips one trailing CR, so a lone-CR stream never delivers an event and a call answered that way fails with "stream ended without a response". Size limits, dispatch and error cleanup are unchanged. The stdio framing (LF-terminated JSON lines, `lineSplitter`) is unchanged.

Why: A server that frames its SSE with a lone CR was unusable.

Pi bug: 1.5.0 parses a CR-framed stream as one `id` line at EOF (#11764).

Scope: the SSE reader of the Streamable HTTP transport and the legacy SSE transport (`mcp/sse.go`, new `sseLineSplitter`). Not affected: stdio.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-05 for 0.7.1 (SCOPE-PROPOSAL S20).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: the pinned Pi release contains b64e6badd. Then the call-site marker and this record go.

Call-site markers: `mcp/sse.go` (`sseLineSplitter`).

Evidence: `TestConsumeSSEStreamRecognizesCRLFAndCRLineEndingsIncludingCRLFSplitAcrossChunks` and `TestConsumeSSEStreamDispatchesCRTerminatedEventsWhileTheStreamStaysOpen` (`mcp/sse_cr_pi_test.go`, Pi's two tests; red on 989927eff4: `"\r" as one chunk: []mcp.SSEEvent(nil), want ...`, the second never dispatches), the existing stdio splitter tests unchanged.

Parity allowance: none needed; Pi 1.5.0 has no CR-only test.

## D262 The startup benchmark disposes the session runtime

What: PiG's `PI_STARTUP_BENCHMARK=1` path ports Pi main efb45b51d (`fix(coding-agent): dispose session runtime after startup benchmark`, #11736, fixing #11693), which is not in Pi 1.5.0. After it initializes interactive mode and stops it, it disposes the session runtime (extensions receive `session_shutdown`), then prints the timings. Pi 1.5.0 never disposes the runtime on this path. PiG also disposes it when initialization or stop fails, before the error exit.

Parity allowance: none needed.

## D266 An image block with an empty `mimeType` is named `image/unknown` in tool cards, where Pi names it with the empty string

What: `getTextOutput` (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/tools/render-utils.ts`) draws a fallback line for each image block the terminal does not show, naming it `img.mimeType ?? "image/unknown"`. A block with no `mimeType` prints `[Image: [image/unknown]]` in Pi and PiG alike. A block whose `mimeType` is the empty string prints `[Image: []]` in Pi and `[Image: [image/unknown]]` in PiG. `ai.ImageContent.MimeType` is a plain string, so PiG treats an empty type as an absent one. The same call in Pi guards the image dimensions with a truthiness test (`img.data && img.mimeType`), which already treats `""` as no type. The difference shows in every card that draws `getTextOutput`: the bash, read, write, grep, find, ls, MCP and codemode result cards.

Why: PiG's output names the image with a type a reader can recognise instead of an empty bracket pair, and keeping absent and empty alike avoids a presence flag on a core public type (`ai.ImageContent`) for one fallback label. Only an extension or MCP server that sends `mimeType: ""` can reach it.

Pi bug: Pi 1.5.0's `??` fallback and its own truthiness guard disagree about `""`; not reported upstream.

Observable effect: Pi's tool card names an image block whose `mimeType` is the empty string `[Image: []]`, an empty bracket pair, where PiG names it `[Image: [image/unknown]]`.

Scope: `tools.GetTextOutput` and `codemodeTextOutput`; image blocks with an empty `mimeType` only. Blocks with a type, or with none, render as in Pi.

Owner decision: 2026-11-06, owner approved keeping PiG's output and recording this as a numbered divergence (D266).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: Pi treats an empty `mimeType` like an absent one in `getTextOutput`.

It fails when either side changes.

## D267 The plugins' `npm install` ends with its command, not with the processes that hold the command's output pipes

What: `InstallDependencies` (`chord/nextnode/plugins`, the port of `installDependencies` in `packages/chord/src/next-node/plugins/install.ts`) waits for the install command to exit, and for the end of its stdout and stderr only for five seconds after that (the stop grace, `exec.Cmd.WaitDelay`). A process the command started that inherited its pipes (a daemon npm left behind) no longer holds the install: it returns with the command's result, the output written so far, and the pipes' Go ends closed (what the child writes later is dropped and its writes fail with a broken pipe). A command that exited with code 0 is installed even when the grace expired. Pi settles the install on the child's `close` event, which fires only when the command's stdio streams have closed, so with a process left holding them Pi's install waits for as long as that process lives.

Why: A plugin install that hangs on a descendant the user cannot see blocks the plugin load behind it with no error and no way out but killing the app; the 0.7.1 fix of the Windows Node-host exit (commit 47ec6e80d8) found the same exposure in the Node host's own process wait and fixed it there (`NodeHost` waits for the process's exit, as Pi's workers do with `child.once("exit")`, `next-node/workers/process.ts:79`, so that path matches Pi and is no divergence). The install is the one place where PiG waits as Pi's `close` does, so it follows the exit instead.

Pi bug: Pi's `installDependencies` hangs until a process that inherited npm's pipes ends (`child.on("close", resolve)`, `install.ts:42`). Not reported upstream.

Scope: `InstallDependencies` only, in the case a process other than the command holds the command's stdout or stderr after the command exited. `WaitDelay` also counts from an abort: when the context ends, the command is sent SIGTERM as before, and a command that ignores it is now killed five seconds later and the install returns the abort's reason then (measured 6.0 s, against 30.1 s on 989927eff4, where `Run` waited for the command to end by itself). Pi rejects at once and sends SIGTERM and does not kill, so the abort path is closer to Pi, not further. Nothing differs when the command's streams close with it (every case Pi's tests cover): arguments, working directory, output text on failure, the abort path and the exit-code messages are Pi's.

Owner decision: 2026-11-05, approved through the coordinator for 0.7.1.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: Pi stops waiting on inherited pipes (its install settles on the command's `exit`, or bounds the wait for `close`). Then the call-site marker and this record go.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_install.go` (`InstallDependencies`, the `WaitDelay` and the `ErrWaitDelay` case).

Evidence: `TestInstallDependenciesEndsWithTheCommandThoughAProcessItStartedHoldsItsPipes` (`chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_install_test.go`; red on 989927eff4: the install did not return in 25 s for exit 0 and for exit 1).

Parity allowance: none needed; Pi's install tests (`install.test.ts`, ported in `plugins_install_test.go`) pass unchanged, and none of them leaves a process holding the pipes.

## D268 The extension SDKs read tool arguments of any depth the host can send; Pi's runtime fails at a depth set by V8's stack

What: Pi's own runtime (the Node SDK) returns a tool's arguments only while V8 has stack for `JSON.stringify`. On Node 24.22.1 with the default stack, arguments shaped `{"zeta":1,"meta":{"d":[...]}}` come back as an object at d=4300 and fail with `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` at d=4400 (`JSON.stringify` fails past 4459 at the default stack and past 9103 with `--stack-size=2000`; plain `JSON.parse` reads any depth). The limit is V8 stack exhaustion: it moves with the call depth, the Node version, the platform and `--stack-size`, so no fixed Pi depth exists to reproduce. PiG's Go, Python, Rust and Lisp SDKs return the object, and the texts Pi writes for it, for every frame the host can send. An argument value can nest 10000 levels (the host's marshal validates it alone against 10000: d=9998 in the row shape above). None of the four ends its extension or its connection on deeper input: a request is answered with the RangeError message, a host call result is an error, a notification is dropped with a line on stderr. The Node SDK is Pi's own runtime; PiG's Node runtime copies each event-stream payload for its handler (iteratively), and a handler receives the object as in Pi, up to where V8's own `JSON.stringify` runs out of stack (about 4400 levels on Node 24).

| SDK | What reads the frame | Bound |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Go | `json.UnmarshalFrame` (`extensions/sdk/json`; `Unmarshal` and the host's marshal keep the standard 10000) | 10064 levels of the whole frame |
| Python | `json.loads`, then a non-recursive parser (`_loads_deep`); ordering and stringify are iterative | 10100 levels (`MAX_FRAME_DEPTH`); `json.loads` alone stops at about 9997 on 3.12 and about 990 on 3.11 |
| Rust | `from_json_str` on a 64 MiB decode stack (the arguments of a request or notification and the operations of an event-stream delta); a request with such a value runs on a 64 MiB thread, the connection's main loop on an 8 MiB one | 10016 levels (`MAX_NESTING_DEPTH`) |
| Lisp | the JSON reader on the image's 64 MiB control stack (kept by every thread: address space reserved with `MAP_NORESERVE`) | 10016 levels (`*max-json-depth*`) |
| Node | Pi's own `JSON.parse` and `JSON.stringify` | V8's stack (about 4400 levels on Node 24) |

Why: Before, an extension could die on arguments Pi's own host sends it. The Go SDK ended its connection from d=9997: its reader validated the whole frame against encoding/json's 10000-level limit, while the host validates a request's arguments alone, so a frame the host sends nests a few levels past it (a limit of the codec, not a stack). The Python SDK failed the call from about 1000 levels, and from about 990 on Python 3.11, because its ordering and `JSON.stringify` were recursive and its JSON reader is bound by the recursion limit. The Rust and Lisp readers ended the connection past 4096 and 2000 levels. Rust's `Context::tool_params` returned none past 128 levels. Rust's event-stream deltas (a `message_update` whose tool call carries deep arguments, a `tool_execution_update`) ended the connection past 128 levels. Matching Pi's observable result for the arguments Pi accepts, and failing a call (never the extension) beyond what an SDK reads, keeps tool calls with legitimately nested JSON working. Pi's exact cut-off is not reproducible.

Pi bug: none; Pi's limit is the stack of the JavaScript engine, not a defect to report.

Scope: arguments, results and events (the whole payload of an event and the operations of a `message_update` or `tool_execution_update` delta) read by the Go, Python, Rust and Lisp SDKs, in every placement (isolated and packed), up to the host's 10000 levels. Not the Node SDK. Not a value nested past 10000 levels (the host refuses to send one).

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-06 (proposed by the 0.7.1 coordinator).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: Pi parses and returns tool arguments without a stack-bound depth limit, or the owner decides the SDKs should stop at Pi's observed depth. Then the call-site markers and this record go.

Call-site markers: `extensions/sdk/protocol.go` (`readLoop`), `extensions/sdk-py/pig_sdk/__init__.py` (`Extension._read`), `extensions/sdk-rs/src/protocol.rs` (`MAX_NESTING_DEPTH`), `extensions/sdk-lisp/src/json.lisp` (`*max-json-depth*`; Lisp is not scanned by `check-divergence-consistency.sh`).

Evidence: `TestToolParamsNestingAcrossSDKs` (`test/extension-conformance/tool_params_deep_test.go`; red on 03c551f3e2 for Go at d=9998, and for Python, Rust and Lisp), `TestModelStreamEventWithDeepToolArgumentsReachesEverySDK` (`model_stream_deep_event_test.go`), `TestDeepEventStreamDeltasReachEverySDK` (`deep_event_delta_test.go`; deep `message_update` and `tool_execution_update` deltas, red for Rust on c1da695c1d), both over isolated and packed placements, and each SDK's own suite for the frames past its bound (`extensions/sdk/protocol_deep_frame_test.go`, `extensions/sdk-py/tests/test_deep_frames.py`, `extensions/sdk-rs/src/surrogate_tests.rs`, `extensions/sdk-lisp/tests/json-tests.lisp`).

Parity allowance: the row pins every SDK's outcome at depths 200, 1000, 4000, 5000, 9000 and 9998: the object for the Go, Python, Rust and Lisp SDKs at all six; for the Node SDK (Pi's own runtime, pinned Node 24.22.1) the object at 200, 1000 and 4000 and V8's RangeError at 5000, 9000 and 9998. The event rows (`TestDeepEventStreamDeltasReachEverySDK`) pin the payload of a `message_update` or `tool_execution_update` delta, in arrays and in objects, at 200, 3000, 4000, 9000 (and 1 after them): every SDK hands its handler the payload, except that the Node SDK's handler at 9000 throws V8's RangeError from its own `JSON.stringify`, as a handler does in Pi, which gives the handler the object without copying. Each depth is followed by a call to the same extension. Pi's runtime gives the object at d=4300 and the RangeError at d=4400 on that Node; the row does not pin those, because the cut-off moves with the Node version.

## D269 A system fd or rg is accepted only if `--version` names it, and the `@` autocomplete runs fd in a new session

What: Pi's tools-manager `commandExists` runs `spawnSync(cmd, ["--version"], { stdio: "pipe" })` and counts any result but a spawn error as "installed", with no timeout, in Pi's own session. `getToolPath("fd")` therefore returns the first of `fd`, `fdfind` that spawns, and Pi runs it at startup (`ensureTool("fd")`) and on `@`. Debian's `fdclone` package installs a file manager as `/usr/bin/fd`; it opens `/dev/tty`, so Pi's `@` autocomplete, `find` and startup hand it the terminal. PiG's base only looked the name up (it never spawned), so it started FDclone at `@` and in `find`. PiG now:

| Step | Pi | PiG |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Candidate names | `fd`, then `fdfind` (rg: `rg`) | same |
| Acceptance of a PATH candidate | `spawnSync(cmd, ["--version"])` returns without a spawn error | `<bin> --version` exits 0 and stdout starts with `fd ` or `fdfind ` (rg: `ripgrep `); Debian's sharkdp build prints `fdfind 10.3.0` |
| On mismatch | the impostor wins | next name, then the managed download in `<agentDir>/bin` |
| The probe | Pi's session, no timeout | started like the searches (`nodespawn.SetProgram`), in a new session on unix or a console of its own without a window on Windows (`nodespawn.HideWindow`), stdin `/dev/null`, stdout a pipe; a 5 s bound (1 s pipe grace) that kills the process group on unix (the process on Windows); a completed verdict is cached per path, mtime and size, a timeout or start failure is not |
| `@` autocomplete fd search | Pi's session | new session on unix (a cancel kills the group); on Windows a console of its own without a window. `DETACHED_PROCESS` is not used: a Scoop or Chocolatey shim started under it opens a visible console window for the real program |
| `find` and `grep` fd/rg search | Pi's session | unchanged: the caller's session and group, so a terminal Ctrl+C in print mode reaches the search with the agent |

The managed copy in `<agentDir>/bin` is PiG's own download and is not probed.

Why: typing `@` over ssh on a host with fdclone launched a full-screen file manager, and a real fd installed as `fdfind` was never reached. Pi has the same bug.

Pi bug: `commandExists` in `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/tools-manager.ts` accepts any program that spawns, so an unrelated `fd` passes. To report upstream.

Scope: `tools.LookupToolPath` and `ToolsManager.GetToolPath` (so `EnsureTool`, `BeginEnsureTool` at startup through `ensureManagedTools`, the tools status line, and the `find`, `grep` and `@` lookups), and the `@` autocomplete's fd run. A system `fd` or `rg` whose `--version` line is not one of the prefixes (a wrapper, a custom build) is no longer used; it falls through to the managed download, or to nothing offline. Narrower than the first proposal: `find` and `grep` do not run their search in a new session, because the identity probe already keeps an impostor out and a new session would orphan rg and fd on a terminal Ctrl+C in print and json modes.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-06 (proposed by the 0.7.1 coordinator).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: Pi checks that the program it found is the tool, not only that it spawns. Then the probe, the autocomplete's new session and this record go.

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/tools/tool_probe.go` (`systemToolIdentity`, the 5 s probe bound, the 1 s pipe grace), `tui/file_autocomplete.go` (`walkDirectoryWithFd`).

Evidence: `TestLookupToolPathSkipsImpostorFdForFdfind`, `TestLookupToolPathRejectsLoneImpostor`, `TestLookupToolPathChecksRipgrepIdentity`, `TestLookupToolPathAcceptsFdWrapperWithoutShebang`, `TestToolProbeCachesPerFileRevision`, `TestProbeToolVersionTimesOutHungProgramAndItsChildren`, `TestProbeToolVersionDoesNotCacheStartFailure`, `TestVersionProbeCannotReachTheTerminal`, `TestFindAndGrepKeepSearchInCallersSession` (`internal/codingagent/tools/tool_probe_unix_test.go`, `tool_probe_linux_test.go`) and `TestWalkDirectoryWithFdRunsInANewSession`, `TestWalkDirectoryWithFdCancelKillsTheGroup` (`tui/file_autocomplete_session_linux_test.go`). The impostor and no-shebang cases fail on a45b578455, where a fake `fd` ahead of `fdfind` was returned.

Parity allowance: `TestLookupToolPathFindsToolsAsPiDoes` keeps comparing lookup order with Pi's `getToolPath` on Windows with fixtures that answer `--version` as the tool, so only the identity check differs.

## D270 After a /tree choice PiG holds its frames until the navigation returns; Pi paints the old transcript first

What: Pi's tree handler (interactive-mode.ts:5694-5775) awaits `session.navigateTree` (5750) and then runs `chatContainer.clear(); renderInitialMessages()` (5768). The key that closes the "Summarize branch?" prompt asks for an immediate frame (tui.ts:1158-1161) that runs on `process.nextTick` (tui.ts:1044-1058), before the microtask that resumes the await. So Pi paints the old transcript with the prompt gone first, and the fork point replaces it on the next frame: a few milliseconds without an extension handler, and the whole await of a `session_before_tree` handler that awaits. PiG's navigation runs on a worker and decodes the session's records for the context: on a 27 MB, 600-entry session of 88 KB tool results that is about 60 ms at 16 cores and about 190 ms at 4 (about 750 ms at 1) after the parallel read splits by record bytes (it was 830 ms at 16 before), and the owner saw the old transcript for about a second before the fork point. PiG now holds its frames from the choice that starts the navigation (No summary, Summarize, the custom prompt's submit, or the tree selection when `branchSummary.skipPrompt` is set) until the navigation has returned and the transcript is swapped, so the prompt stays on screen and the first frame after the choice is the fork point. The transcript's entries are read on the worker, so the repaint decodes nothing on the owner loop.

Why: the owner asked that the user never see the old leaf after selecting. Pi's old-transcript frame is brief enough not to be seen; PiG's decode is not always brief, and a hold is the only way to show no stale frame whatever the navigation takes.

Pi bug: none.

Scope: `/tree` navigation. The hold starts only from the choice of the prompt's own dialog. It ends when the navigation returns, when an extension opens a dialog or an overlay, when a summary shows its status, and when the run is aborted before navigating. Esc and the custom-prompt choice are not held. While it lasts, nothing that only requests a frame is painted: the user's typing, `setStatus`, `setWidget`, footer and header changes. A paint that names a render (`showStatus`, `showError`, notifications) still paints the old transcript with the prompt gone. The hold has no upper bound: a handler that awaits for a minute keeps the "Summarize branch?" prompt on screen for a minute, where Pi shows the old transcript.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-06 (proposed by the 0.7.1 coordinator).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: PiG's navigation completes within one frame for every session, or the owner decides to match Pi's brief old-transcript frame.

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/interactive_tree.go` (`navigateTree`), `internal/codingagent/interactive.go` (`holdFrames`), `internal/codingagent/slash_session_handlers.go` (`treeNavigateWithSummarize`), `internal/codingagent/ext_ui_context.go` (`runDialog`).

Evidence: `TestTreeNavigationPaintsNoStaleFrameBeforeTheTranscriptSwap` (red without the hold: the editor painted during the navigation), `TestTreeSummaryPromptChoiceHoldsTheFramesOfItsClose`, `TestTreeDialogsOfOtherContextsNeverStartTheHold`, `TestTreeNavigationPaintsAnExtensionDialog` and `TestTreeNavigationPaintsAnExtensionOverlay` (`internal/codingagent/interactive_tree_frames_test.go`); scenario `tree/15-tree-navigation-no-stale-frame` (fails 42 of 119 sampled panes with `holdFrames` a no-op).

Parity allowance: scenario `tree/15-tree-navigation-no-stale-frame` runs a `session_before_tree` handler that awaits 600 ms. Both programs end on the same pane (`output_equal`). PiG's pane, sampled every 10 ms from the choice until "Navigated to selected point" shows, never shows the old leaf with the prompt gone (`pig_no_stale_frames`); Pi's samples are not asserted, because Pi shows that pane throughout the await.

## D310 `/reload` and a session switch remove every extension overlay

What: PiG ports Pi main 80d4d3c86 (`fix(coding-agent): remove all extension overlays on /reload and session switch`, #11853), which is not in Pi 1.5.0. `/reload` and a session switch remove every overlay an extension opened with `ctx.ui.custom({ overlay: true })`, hidden ones included, newest first, and dispose their components; pi's own overlays stay open. Pi 1.5.0 removed only the topmost overlay, so the lower ones stayed on screen and kept focus and input after the extension that opened them was gone.

Not affected: inline custom components, which the dialog stack already resets.

Owner decision: owner sign-off pending (draft; Pi main follow of 2026-11-07).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: the pinned Pi release contains 80d4d3c86. Then the call-site markers and this record go.

What: PiG ports Pi main acd059b72 (`fix(coding-agent): apply ctrl+o to user bash output shown while the agent is working`, #11886, fixing #11875), which is not in Pi 1.5.0. The output of `!` and `!!` commands run while the agent works starts in the current expand state, stays in the pending-message area when a steering or follow-up message is queued, and moves into the chat when the run settles instead of on the next submitted message. ## D352 Anthropic message threads

What: PiG ports Anthropic message threads (`ai/anthropic_threads.go`): with a session id and cache retention other than none, requests continue from the previous reply and send only new messages, a rejected continue is resent in full as a new thread, and an endpoint that rejects threads turns them off for the session. `compat.supportsMessageThreads` overrides the default (on for provider `anthropic` on api.anthropic.com), and `StreamOptions.DisableContinuation` keeps a request off thread state (the cache warmer sets it). Pi 1.5.0 has none of this. Ports Pi main a758f3ae4 (`feat(ai): Anthropic message threads`, #11545), which is not in Pi 1.5.0.

## D370 The OAuth callback server drops connections that have not sent a request when it closes

What: PiG ports Pi main 63ea042ce (`fix(mcp): close remaining connections when the OAuth callback server closes`, #11855, fixing #11311), which is not in Pi 1.5.0. Pi calls `server.closeAllConnections()`; Go has no equivalent that spares a response being written, so `OAuthCallbackServer.Close` closes the connections that never sent a request (a browser's preconnect) and leaves idle and in-flight ones to `Shutdown`.

Why: Go's `Shutdown` waits up to 5 s for a connection that never sent a request, so `/mcp login` stalled up to the 2 s bound.

Pi bug: 1.5.0 waits for the browser to drop the socket, for minutes.

Owner decision: owner sign-off pending.

## D430 Go callers start a call and await it apart (Reference.Start, PendingCall) and run a handler's blocking remainder from outside the host package (AwaitingCtx)

What: Pi's `ref.method(args)` starts the call at once and returns a Promise. A caller may start several calls before awaiting any (Files v2's pipelined `append`s, which the call window allows) and the host accepts them in the order they were started. A blocking Go `Reference.Call` cannot express that, so `Reference.Start(method, args, ctx)` is the synchronous half (the call is admitted and its handler's synchronous part has run when it returns) and `PendingCall.Wait(ctx)` the awaiting half. `host.AwaitingCtx(ctx, work)` is `Awaiting` for a caller outside the package, which cannot name the host's loop: the blocking remainder of an `onLive` or `on` handler (the awaits of Pi's async function).

Why: AGENTS.md "TypeScript async/Promise parity": an awaited Promise is a blocking call, but a Promise started and awaited later is two Go operations. Without Start the pipelined-append behaviour could not be tested or used through a reference; without AwaitingCtx a package outside `chord/next/host` (the app kit) could not declare an `onLive` routine.

Pi bug: none.

Scope: additive Go API of `chord/next/host`; no Pi behaviour changes.

Owner decision: owner sign-off pending (draft; Pi main follow of 2026-11-07).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: never; it is the Go form of an un-awaited Promise and of a handler's async remainder.

Call-site markers: `chord/next/host/host_pending.go`, `chord/nextnode/app/files.go` (`FilesFacet`), `chord/nextnode/app/files_support_test.go`.

Evidence: `TestChunkedUploadsWriteAppendsSentWithoutWaitingAtTheirOwnOffsetsByteForByte` and `TestChunkedUploadsFinishAnUploadOnlyOnceTheAppendsSentBeforeItLanded` (`chord/nextnode/app/files_test.go`), `TestChunkedUploadsDropTheUploadsAPreviousRunLeft` (the `onLive` routine).

Parity allowance: none.

## D502 `TestPlugin` tests a plugin on the production topology: its host is the Node subprocess and the stubs are Go facets, not JavaScript values handed to one in-process host

What: Pi's `testPlugin(dir, { kind, modules, stubs, ... })` (`packages/chord/src/node/plugins/testing.ts`) builds the plugin, evaluates it in the test's own process with live JavaScript `modules`, loads it with live `stubs` (`PluginFacet[]`) on one new in-memory host and returns that host. PiG's `TestPlugin` (`chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_testing.go`) builds with the same `BuildFacet` and options and the same discovery, but a plugin evaluates only in the Node subprocess (AGENTS.md: no embedded JavaScript runtime; `docs/plan/chord-plugins-node-evaluator.md`), so: `Modules` are files the subprocess imports (the app's API as modules on disk, as a production host's `HostPackages`), not instances; a stub is a Go facet on a Go host (`TestPluginOptions.Stubs`, bridged to the plugin's host as `BridgedFacet` is, so it serves local types without keyed members) or a JavaScript module (`TestPluginOptions.Core`, which may also serve or contribute to registries and serve remote types); the result holds two hosts, the plugin's `NodeHost` (`TestedPlugin.Host`, reached through `NodeHost.Get` and `NodeHost.Hold`, the Go forms of `host.get` and `host.hold` for a local type) and the stubs' Go host (`TestedPlugin.Stubs`).

Why: a live JavaScript closure, module instance or `Host` cannot cross the process boundary, and the alternative (a second evaluator of plugins in the Go process) is forbidden. The topology is the one apps run (a Go host with a Node host as its child), so a plugin that passes `TestPlugin` meets its stubs through the same bridge it meets Go core facets through in production.

Scope: `TestPlugin`, `TestPluginOptions`, `TestStub`, `TestedPlugin.Host` and `Stubs`; the cases of `testing.test.ts` and example 52 give the same inputs (plugin files, API, contracts) and the same expectations (values read, contributions, names listed, errors) through these forms. Behaviour that is Pi's: a facet that throws while it evaluates rejects with that error as it is (`*NodeError`), and a setup that throws is `testPlugin: setup failed: ...` (`TestTestPluginRejectsAScriptThatThrowsWhileItEvaluatesWithItsOwnError`); the build and its refusals (`failed to build`), the versioned-API check, the root and kind derivation (single-file and checkout plugins, kinds named by files, `*.test.*`/`*.d.ts` naming none), the collecting facet and its fixed point, the wait for activations bounded by `timeouts.ready`, an in-memory store.

Owner decision: pending sign-off (option (a), port with a Go `TestPlugin`, 2026-11-08 00:36Z; this record is the residue).

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_testing.go` (`TestStub`, `TestPlugin`), `chord/nextnode/plugins/node/testing.mjs`.

Evidence: `TestTestPlugin*` (`chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_testing_test.go`, the cases of `testing.test.ts`) and `TestExample52TestPlugin` (`plugins_examples_testplugin_test.go`).

Parity allowance: none needed beyond the forms above; the Pi cases pass with Go stubs, file modules and the two hosts.

Remove when: Pi evaluates plugins out of process (its stubs then need a wire form too) or PiG gets an in-process evaluator.

## D503 `TestPlugin`'s registries are named, its contributions are data, and `root` is `RootPrincipal`: no JavaScript closure or registry token crosses

What: Pi's `TestedPlugin.contributions(R)` takes the registry token `R` and returns its entries with their live values, and `root(fn, context, options)` runs the closure `fn` as the host's principal or `options.principal`. PiG's `TestedPlugin.Contributions(name, ctx)` takes the registry's name and returns `[]Contribution{Plugin, Type, Version, Value}` with each value as JSON data (a function or other non-data value in a contribution does not cross; the control channel's value form carries `bytes()` leaves), and `RootPrincipal(principal, ctx)` runs the fixed closure `async (ctx) => ctx.principal` of Pi's own test on the plugin's host and answers the principal its context has (the host's own for nil). The error for a registry that is not collected is Pi's message (`testPlugin: registry <name> is not collected ...`).

Why: the plugin's host is in the Node subprocess; a Go function cannot run there as an object's code, and a JavaScript closure written in a Go test is not a Go value. The helper's own reads (the collection's fixed point over `undeclaredRegistries`, `activationsUnderWay`, `timeouts.ready`, `unservedNames`, the reload that adds the collecting facet) run in the subprocess (`node/testing.mjs`), so the host's internals never cross; only their results do.

Scope: `TestedPlugin.Contributions`, `RootPrincipal`, `Unserved`. A test that needs `ctx.get` of other objects as another principal has no Go form of `root` yet; it reads through `NodeHost.Get` as the host's principal.

Owner decision: pending sign-off (same option as D502).

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_testing.go` (`Contributions`, `RootPrincipal`), `chord/nextnode/plugins/node/testing.mjs`.

Evidence: `TestTestPluginLoadsThePluginBuiltAgainstTheAPIBesideAStubCollectsItsContributionsAndListsWhatItWaitsFor` (`plugins_testing_test.go`: contributions by name, root as the host and as `u2`), `TestExample52TestPlugin`.

Parity allowance: none needed.

Remove when: PiG's plugin host can run a Go closure in the plugin's host process, or Pi's `root` takes data.

## D540 The plugins watcher's directory identity has no birth time on a system whose stat Go does not expose it for

What: Pi renews the base watches under an entry on the way to base code only when the entry's identity, its inode and birth time (`identityOf`, `watcher.ts`), differs from the one it had when watched. PiG does the same with `statx` (ino, btime) on Linux and Android, `lstat` (ino, birthtimespec) on macOS, FreeBSD and NetBSD, `lstat` (ino, `X__st_birthtim`) on OpenBSD, and the file index and creation time on Windows. On any other Unix (DragonFly, Solaris and illumos, AIX) Go's `syscall.Stat_t` has no birth time, so the identity is the inode alone: a directory removed and made again that is given the same inode number is not seen as replaced there.

Why: the birth time is not in Go's `syscall` on those systems, and nothing in PiG ships for them (the release matrix is Linux, Android, macOS and Windows). Linux file systems with no birth time (tmpfs) give 0 in Pi as well (libuv's `statx`), so they are not part of this record.

Pi bug: Not a Pi bug.

Scope: `chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_watcher_identity_other.go`, the `base` mark of `pluginWatcher` on DragonFly, Solaris and illumos, and AIX.

Owner decision: owner sign-off pending (drafted by the FollowChordPlugins lane on 2026-11-08).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: PiG ships for such a system and its `Stat_t` carries a birth time, or Go's `syscall` gives one.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_watcher_identity_other.go`.

Evidence: `TestIdentityOfADirectoryIsItsInodeAndBirthTimeAndNoneForNoEntry` states the identity on the systems tested; the build of the file is checked by `GOOS=dragonfly go vet ./chord/nextnode/plugins` (and netbsd, openbsd for the files that read a birth time).

Parity allowance: none needed; no Pi test depends on an inode reused with no birth time.

## D600 A Go worker's base is a private copy of its own executable, not an esbuild bundle: no bundle, no `cache/node`, no `NODE_COMPILE_CACHE`, and the copy is made at the build

What: Pi builds a worker kind's base by bundling the entry module with the base code it imports (`workers/base.ts`, esbuild) in memory, writes it at the first fork into a private directory its component owns (the kit's `cache/workers/<kind>/`, emptied first, removed at `close` after every worker exited, never touched by a component that closed), records every file the build read (`WorkersBuilt.inputs`) and forks its workers with `NODE_COMPILE_CACHE` set to the kit's `cache/node/`. PiG's worker is the program the app names (`Entry`, usually its own executable), which holds its code already. `Workers.Build` (run by the kit right after the server listens) checks that `Entry` can be started, copies it into `WorkersOptions.BaseDir` (the kit's `cache/workers/<kind>/`, emptied first; a new temporary directory without one) and records the entry with the hash of the bytes copied; every worker forks that copy; `Close` removes the directory once every worker exited (also when its context ends the wait first), and a component that closed before its build copies nothing and leaves a named directory as it is. A copy that is gone (a cleaner of temporary files) is made again from the entry when the entry still has the recorded bytes. An edit of the entry marks `base` (the kit watches the recorded file), and runs in no worker. The layout has `cache/` and `cache/workers/` (mode 0700), not `cache/node/`.

Why: a program cannot be held in memory as Pi's bundle is, so the snapshot is a file made at the build, not at the first fork: it keeps what Pi's test pins ("a worker started after an edit of the entry's imported source runs the code the base was built with"). There is no module graph to bundle, no Node compile cache for a compiled executable, and no esbuild error to report: the Go build error is an entry that cannot be started or copied.

Pi bug: Not a Pi bug.

Scope: `chord/nextnode/workers` (`Workers.Build`, `WorkersBuilt`, `WorkersOptions.BaseDir`), `chord/nextnode/app` (`AppLayout.Cache`, `AppLayout.WorkerBases`, the worker kinds' base records).

Owner decision: owner sign-off pending (drafted by the ChordKit284 lane on 2026-11-08).

Remove when: PiG's worker is a Node entry again.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/workers/workers_workers.go` (`Build`), `chord/nextnode/workers/doc.go`.

Evidence: `TestTheWorkerBaseAWorkerStartedAfterAnEditOfTheEntrysExecutableRunsTheCodeTheBaseWasBuiltWith`, `TestTheWorkerBaseAComponentThatClosedBeforeItsBuildWritesNothingAndLeavesANamedDirectoryAsItIs`, `TestTheWorkerBaseInANamedDirectoryEmptiedFirstWrittenAgainThereRemovedByClose`, `TestTheWorkerBaseABrokenEntryFailsItsBuildAndEveryStartWorkerFailedThePublicStartErrorNothingForks` (`chord/nextnode/workers`), `TestAppMarksBaseForAChangeOfAWorkerKindsEntryNotForARewriteWithTheSameBytes`, `TestAppReportsABaseThatFailsAfterTheListenAndRunsOn…` (`chord/nextnode/app`).

Parity allowance: Pi's `base.test.ts` and the worker-base tests of `workers.test.ts` that bundle a Node module, the `NODE_COMPILE_CACHE` and `cache/node` assertions of `layout.test.ts` and `app.test.ts`, and the `app.test.ts` tests that hold a worker kind's base build on a pipe have no counterpart.

## D604 A link's upgrade waits for the bases to be built (Pi's links connect at once)

What: with a web or terminal surface, Pi's server listens, then builds the bases; the scripts object shows an empty set (`base: ""`) until the first set is published, on the assumption that no client holds it then. PiG's `StartServer` returns when the server listens, and its links endpoint (`LinksOptions.OptionsFunc`) holds each upgrade until `BasesBuilt` (or the app's close, which refuses it) before the link is accepted. Plain HTTP requests (the owner page, the API) are answered meanwhile as in Pi; a page or surface file waits for its own base.

Why: the Go server listens before the plugins' Node host has loaded (about 0.5 s), and a page of a restarted server reconnects in that gap, before the bases have started. It then holds the empty set, its base differs from the page's, and the page reloads (`Outdated`): a seamless reconnect becomes a reload, and Pi's ported test of a failed plugin script tried again at a reconnect fails every time. Pi's build takes about 100 ms after a start of a few milliseconds, so the window is rarely open there.

Observable effect: a base build that hangs holds every link of the app (pages, terminal clients, objects that need no surface), where Pi's link connects at once.

Pi bug: Not a Pi bug.

Scope: `chord/nextnode/app` (`App.linksAfterTheBases`).

Owner decision: owner sign-off pending (drafted by the ChordKit284 lane on 2026-11-08). A narrower form (the two scripts objects waiting for the build, through the host's dependency parking) is possible and would let the other links connect at once.

Remove when: the Go server's start no longer leaves a window (the plugins' host loads before the listen), or the scripts objects wait for the build themselves.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/app/app_bases.go` (`linksAfterTheBases`).

Evidence: `TestAPagesPluginStylesheetsTryAFailedPluginsScriptAgainAtAReconnectWithItsStylesheetNeverOneWithoutTheOther` (red without the wait: the page reloaded), `TestAppHoldsALinkUntilTheBasesAreBuilt`.

Parity allowance: none needed.

## D605 The server's two hosts serve one union: `Host.Delegate` one way, the Node host's destination the other

What: Pi's server runs its plugins in its own host (`createHost({ address: Server() })` loads them), so the server serves the union of the kit's types and the plugins' from one place. The Go kit's plugins run in a Node subprocess behind the Go server host, which is also addressed `server` (it serves the kit's own remote types: the locale and theme choices, telemetry ingest, the app's Go facets). Both directions are kept so a hold binds on whichever of the two serves the type. Go to Node: `Host.Delegate(kind, RouteOptions, ctx)` is the route of the host's own address kind, consulted only for remote types placed on the host that its published table does not serve: those holds and references go over the delegate (a relayed hold, a remote object, parked while `Resolve` runs, resolved again once after a drop), everything the host serves stays here whatever the state of its object. A hold arriving on the delegate's own link for a type nobody serves is answered `unavailable` (not sent back). The app kit gives the NodeHost the address `server` and delegates to its link instead of accepting it. Node to Go: Pi's `Destination` places every remote type of the NodeHost's own address here, served or not, so a plugin's `self.get(LocaleChoice, {})` would wait `<type> is not served here` for ever. The Node host runs Pi's `Destination` through `node/destination.mjs` (host.ts's import of `./destination.ts` is answered by `resolve.mjs`; Pi's file is vendored as it is): for a remote type placed here that the Go host serves (`Host.Serves`, asked over a synchronous callback, `RuntimeLoad.ParentServes`, kept until the next load or reload) the reference goes over the parent link, where the Go host's own object, whatever its variants and members, binds. Residual difference: a hold over that link acts as the link's principal, the server's (a child cannot claim to be someone else, link §4: `callerOf`), where Pi's one host acts as the plugin object's own principal. So a plugin object's LocaleChoice or ThemeChoice is the server principal's, not the page owner's. Making it the object's principal needs the child's link to name whom a hold acts for, which Pi's protocol allows only from a parent to its child; it is not done here and the test pins today's behaviour.

Why: process boundary (a Node subprocess for the plugins) and the Go server's two hosts (D462). Without the first direction a TypeScript server plugin cannot serve a remote type (`a host without an address serves only local types`, or, with the address, `unavailable`), which every reload row on the server needs; without the second a server plugin cannot hold any type the Go server serves.

Pi bug: none.

Scope: `chord/next/host` (`Host.Delegate`, `Host.Serves`, `destination`, the closure rule's placement by the staged table), `chord/nextnode/plugins` (`node/destination.mjs`, `node/resolve.mjs`, `RuntimeLoad.ParentServes`), `chord/nextnode/app` (`start`). Nothing changes for a host without a delegate, or a NodeHost without `ParentServes`.

Owner decision: owner sign-off pending (draft).

Remove when: never for the two hosts; the principal difference goes when a child's link can name whom a hold acts for.

Call-site markers: `chord/next/host/host_host.go` (`Delegate`, `Serves`), `chord/nextnode/app/app.go` (`start`), `chord/nextnode/plugins/node/destination.mjs`, `chord/next/host/host_delegate_test.go`.

Evidence: `TestDelegateServesTheUnionOfTheHostsTypes...`, `TestDelegateWhatTheDelegatesOwnLinkAsksForAndNothingServesIsNotSentBackToIt`, `TestDelegateWhenItIsDownItsTypesWaitAndTheHostsOwnAreUntouched`, `TestDelegateAHoldMadeWhileItIsDownWaitsOnTheResolvesErrorAndBindsOnceItIsBack`, `TestDelegateHoldsKeepTheirOrderAcrossTheDelegatesRestart...`, `TestDelegateIsRefusedFor...`, `TestDelegateTheClosureRuleReadsTheStagedTable...`, `TestHostServesTheTypesOfItsPublishedTableAndNoOtherName` (`chord/next/host/host_delegate_test.go`), `TestAServerPluginsObjectHoldsTheTypesTheGoServerServesOverTheNodeHostsLinkAsTheServer` (`chord/nextnode/app/app_delegate_test.go`: binds, calls, the principal difference), and the server rows of the reload suite (`chord/nextnode/app/reload_*_test.go`).

Parity allowance: none.
